### Review closure (r7 → CT6) — preserved from r7

Ledger `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/review-crit-r6.txt`, verdict REVISE; IDs C1, C2, E3 (reopened), C3 as assigned by that ledger itself (distinct from the CR2 ledger's same-named ids); severity per the authoritative ledger text. Classes per `workflow-policy/review-findings-taxonomy.md`.

| Finding | Class | Resolution in r7 |
|-------|-------|------------------|
| C1 — Bootstrap can admit a different configuration than its resources use: the initial load at `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve.go:139` configures storage/listeners (`:154`, `:317-324`), then `internal/service/loop.go:137` loads again while published settings remain nil; an intervening A→B edit can admit, persist and report B while the resources still use A | HIGH (lifecycle-hazard) | **Closed — original fixture repaired; failure class resolved.** Verified at r7 (§3): the `load` closure is invoked twice before the first publication — serve's bootstrap call (`candidate, err := load()`, serve.go:139, whose `settings := holder.settings()` at :143 feeds `storage.Open` at :154, the metrics/diagnostics wiring at :145-152, and the listener binds at :317-324) and `Loop.Start`'s call (`l.options.Load()`, loop.go:137) — with `holder.publishedSettings()` nil at both entries, so nothing discriminates them, and `watch`/refresher drivers start only after the startup publication (loop.go:172→:180/:195), so no reload can intervene. **AD21 defines ONE bootstrap snapshot:** the cmd-owned `load` closure memoizes the first successful startup load's effective snapshot (candidate, settings, validated active bytes, source attribution, resolved credential values — the AD22 scan inputs) while the holder's published settings remain nil; every subsequent `load` call in that window returns the memoized snapshot WITHOUT disk I/O. Consequence: the loop's startup admission, publication, pending load-outcome slot, `config_loaded`/`config_degraded` emission, last-known-good persistence and the bootstrap-configured resources all use the SAME generation — an inter-load A→B edit is never admitted, persisted or reported. Cmd-only (the memo lives in the existing closure in serve.go, T3's set); no `internal/service` change (R8; a `LoopOptions` bootstrap field would be one — rejected alternative (k)). Reloads (published non-nil at load entry) never touch the memo — they load from disk under the restart-only guard exactly as today (I4); `--check-config` untouched (AD11); the memoized call re-stages the identical holder state and outcome slot (idempotent, I12/G1 discipline preserved). New invariant I13; fixtures: AC14 `TestStartupInterLoadMutationEmitsBootstrapGeneration` (normal) and AC6(c) `TestDegradedStartInterLoadMutationKeepsBootstrapCopy` (degraded) — **deterministic since r8 via the F2 test-gate seam (see the r8 table)**; a subsequent SIGHUP presenting B is rejected by the restart-only guard (`storage.directory`/`http.listen` are restart-only, wiring.go:100-123) with no emission. Adjacent cases checked: mutation landing before the bootstrap load (fixture race) still yields a self-consistent generation — the bootstrap read defines it; mutation after publication is ordinary reload traffic under the guard; strict/read-error/fail-closed branches return before any memo exists (unchanged). Alternative rejected (j): fingerprint-compare-and-fail-closed at the loop's load — it converts a benign mid-startup edit into a new startup failure class |
| C2 — Raw-copy secret safety does not follow from credential-field validation: YAML comments remain accepted (`internal/config/fingerprint_test.go:83-91`), so a valid document referencing an environment token can also contain that token in a comment; exact-byte persistence copies it, violating R7, while AC8's ordinary reference fixture can pass | HIGH (ac-not-falsifiable) | **Closed — failure class resolved; R7 unchanged (enforced, not weakened).** Verified at r7 (§3): `fingerprint_test.go:83-91` proves presentation-only comments survive parsing, so the r6 §3/AD2 claim "a raw byte copy of a validated document cannot contain resolved secret material" was FALSE — validation rejects inline credential values in credential FIELDS (config.go:809, :1078), not in comments or arbitrary noncredential text. **AD22: the persisted copy's secret safety is ENFORCED by a pre-persist scan, not inferred from validation.** Before any persistence attempt, the exact validated active bytes are scanned with byte containment (`bytes.Contains`) for EVERY resolved credential value the document's own references resolve to — each configured server's `ServerCredentials(serverID)` (`Credentials{Bearer string, Headers http.Header}`, credentials.go:42) and `EmbeddingCredential()` (credentials.go:141), both exported and cmd-callable today (`configuredServerIDs`, wiring.go:592); only successfully resolved, non-empty values are scanned. On ANY match: skip the persistence attempt entirely and emit exactly one typed, sanitized `config_last_good_write_failed` warn record — reusing the EXISTING family — never the matched bytes or any secret. **The CR7 F1 amendment added the client-token refusal layer to the same gate (see the r8 table); the r9/C4 repair REMOVES that refusal layer and extends the scan to the resolved client-token values via the owner-widening-3 exported resolver — AD22 remains the single pre-persist gate, now two-way (credential-scan skip, I/O failure — one warn-record family).** AC8 gained the comment and noncredential-text negatives at r7; the client-token negative lands at r8 and is corrected at r9 (`TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnClientTokenInComment`) |
| E3 (reopened) — AC17 still could not establish the approved live-evidence requirement: ticket `issues/02-diagnose-and-fix-network-startup-loop.md:9` requires the typed diagnostic FROM the live instance, while r6 permitted local capture plus a PENDING interpretation marker; acknowledging the conflict preserved provenance but did not close the acceptance mismatch | HIGH (statement-contradiction) | **Closed — original E3 fixture resolved by a bound owner decision, not by restating the ticket.** The owner decision artifact `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/owner-ticket02-evidence.json` (schemaVersion 1, feature `config-admission-resilience`, revision r7, decision `approve-ticket02-evidence-lane`, origin `user`, explicit true, timedOut false, automatic false, recordedAt 2026-11-22T05:10:00Z, answer **`approve`**) APPROVES the evidence lane: "R2's typed stage/cause is captured by running the T1 build against the live instance's actual config/state before the fix; live running/restart proof remains post-fix owner/operator evidence. No T2 deploy/publish/network effect is granted." AD20 is rewritten from PENDING to APPROVED with the artifact cited; R2/AC17 drop the PENDING marker and record the approved decision reference; the ticket records the approved substitution plus the live instance's literal generic line and `RestartCount` evidence verbatim as owner-supplied material. Boundary preserved: the approval binds the pre-fix LOCAL capture lane only — it grants no T2 deploy/publish/network/SSH effect (AD18 unchanged) and no live pre-fix quote; AC18 post-fix live verification remains owner/operator-lane evidence, coordinator-bound. If the owner later revokes the decision, the pre-fix live quote becomes an owner-lane step exactly like AC18 with no packet effect change |
| C3 — T4 was instructed to write outside its fence: r6 said T4 "writes the record into its ticket file" for the AD19 Dockerfile follow-up, but ticket 04 is in NO task write set (§10; canonical state) | MEDIUM (ledger-state-drift) | **Closed.** The instruction is removed everywhere it appeared (AD19, R6, the E2 row annotation, T4's objective/decision-log, §10): **T4 returns the AD19 follow-up verbatim through its task receipt; the coordinator records it into the ticket-04 file (`.scratch/config-admission-resilience/issues/…04…`) in the coordinator's designated lane.** T4 writes NO path outside its 10-path set (unchanged — no ticket path, no `Dockerfile`); no set change, no widening. The receipt is the durable transport; the coordinator's ticket-04 record is the durable artifact. AD19 itself remains PENDING, unchanged |

### Review closure (r8 → CR7) — preserved from r8

Ledger `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/review-cr-r7.txt`, verdict REQUEST CHANGES; IDs F1–F9 assigned by that ledger in ledger order (F6–F9 carry the CR6 advisies F/G/H/I forward); severity per the authoritative ledger text. Classes per `workflow-policy/review-findings-taxonomy.md`. Causality: F1 is an existing spec defect of the r7 AD22 (a coverage gap in the secret-hygiene enforcement), not a repair-induced regression; F2–F9 are falsifiability/consistency defects of the r7 spec text and fixtures.

| Finding | Class | Resolution in r8 (status at r9) |
|-------|-------|------------------|
| F1 — AD22 scanned only `ServerCredentials` + `EmbeddingCredential`, but `client_auth.bearer_tokens` are also secret references (config.go:924-933), resolved then hashed with only `ClientTokenHashes()` exported (config.go:433; digest population in the resolution pass) — a validated document quoting the resolved client token in a comment/noncredential text would be persisted verbatim, violating R7/AC8, and the r7 claim "no `internal/config` change needed" was false as stated | HIGH (secret-hygiene / ac-not-falsifiable) | **Closed at r8 by the refusal layer — the coverage gap itself (client tokens are scannable secret material) is re-resolved at r9/C4 by the SCAN path.** Verified at r8 (§3): the client-token resolution pass resolves each `client_auth.bearer_tokens` entry to its value and stores only its sha256 digest in the unexported `clientTokenHashes` field (config.go:68; `ClientTokenHashes()` at config.go:433 was the ONLY exported surface) — the resolved client-token VALUES were never exposed to cmd, so no scan input for them could exist without an `internal/config` export, which the r8 packet did not have. **AD22 (r8): the pre-persist gate REFUSED persistence outright whenever `client_auth.bearer_tokens` was configured** — skipping the write and emitting the sanitized `config_last_good_write_failed` warn record (client-token refusal class), so R7 held by refusal. **r9/C4 (CT8): the refusal layer is REMOVED — it weakened ticket-03's persistence contract; the owner approved widening 3, the scan layer now includes the resolved client-token values via the exported `Config.ResolvedClientTokens()` resolver, and the clean-token positive + token-in-comment negative fixtures enforce both sides (see the r9 table). The secret-hygiene closure stands — enforced by scan, not refusal. (r10/H5 anchor correction: `ClientTokenHashes()` is declared at `internal/config/config.go:81`; `:433` is the digest-append line inside the resolution pass — see the r10 table.)** |
| F2 — AC6(c)/AC14 asserted an A→B inter-load mutation "landing after the inbound listener binds" — the listener binds at serve.go:317-324, the loop goroutine at :339; no test seam holds the window, the mutation normally lands after loop.go:137, and both fixtures could pass with the memo removed | HIGH (ac-not-falsifiable) | **Closed — original fixture repaired; unchanged at r9.** **AD21 (amended): a deterministic test-only load-gate seam in `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve.go`** — a cmd-package-level hook variable (nil in production — a no-op then, no timing dependency) invoked at the entry of every `load` closure call, given a boolean telling whether a bootstrap snapshot is already memoized (true exactly on the loop's second pre-publication load). **Hook lifetime:** a fixture assigns the gate before starting serve/`Loop.Start`; the gate blocks the memoized (second) call on a channel until the fixture releases it after mutating the active file; the fixture resets the hook to nil via `t.Cleanup` — the hook exists only for the test's lifetime and is absent from every production path. The fixtures assert the memo directly: **normal (AC14, `TestStartupInterLoadMutationEmitsBootstrapGeneration`)** — valid A on disk; the gate holds the loop's load; the fixture rewrites the active file to B **(which changes `storage.directory` — F3)** and releases the gate; asserted: exactly one `config_loaded` naming A's path and **A's** resolved absolute storage directory (the bootstrap generation), A's validated bytes persisted (AD22 gates permitting), B never admitted/published/reported — and **the memo-falsifier**: with the memo removed the second load reads the disk, picks up B, and the emitted record carries B's storage directory — the assertion fails. **Degraded (AC6(c), `TestDegradedStartInterLoadMutationKeepsBootstrapCopy`)** — invalid active A, valid copy C; the gate holds the loop's load; the fixture rewrites the active file (to a valid B') and releases; asserted: served settings are C's, exactly one `config_degraded` naming A's active rejection and one `config_loaded` naming the COPY path, no persistence (AD9) — and the memo-falsifier: with the memo removed the second load would read valid B', no degraded branch would run, and the copy-path/`config_degraded` assertions fail. Both under `-race`; the gate blocks one load only (the memoized call), so the window is held open deterministically rather than raced. **(r10/H4: the gate boolean is `memo != nil && holder.publishedSettings() == nil` — NOT a bare "already memoized" flag: the memo is never cleared, so a bare `memo != nil` would also be true on the post-publication SIGHUP reload that AC6(c)/AC14 expect to be guard-rejected, hanging those fixtures. The conjoined predicate is true exactly on the loop's second pre-publication load and false on every post-publication load; see the r10 table.)** |
| F3 — AC14 asserted B's SIGHUP is guard-rejected "(`storage.directory` restart-only)" without stating that B changes a restart-only field; a B differing only in a non-restart-only key would be admitted | MEDIUM (ac-evidence-mismatch) | **Closed; unchanged at r9.** AC14's fixture B is stated explicitly: **B = A with a changed `storage.directory`** (a restart-only field, wiring.go:100-123). The same B serves both oracles: the bootstrap-memo falsifier (B's storage directory must NOT appear in the emitted `config_loaded` — the memo holds) and the post-publication SIGHUP case — a SIGHUP presenting B after publication is rejected by the existing restart-only guard (`restart_required`): report `rejected`/`invalid_configuration`, no second `config_loaded`, no `config_degraded`, no copy overwrite. The guard rejection is the asserted behavior because B changes `storage.directory`; no admission of B is possible under any memo state |

| F6 (carried CR6-F) — AC14 said "All under `-race`" but its command lacked `-race` | LOW | **Closed; unchanged at r9.** AC14's verification command is `go test -race ./cmd/mcp-bridge -run '…' -count=1` (the `-race` flag added; pattern unchanged, single `\|` alternation); AC6's inter-load command carries `-race` likewise; **at r9 the inter-load/emission fixtures move to T3's command set, which carries `-race` likewise (T3 validation command 1)** |

### Review closure (r9 → CT8) — preserved from r9

Ledger: the CT8 critic review (verdict **REVISE**, findings C4, C5; coordinator-persisted at `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/review-crit-r8.txt`, raw review record `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/review-ct-r8-raw.json`); severity per the authoritative ledger text. Classes per `workflow-policy/review-findings-taxonomy.md`. Causality: both findings are defects of the r8 spec text/schedule, not repair-induced regressions of landed code — C4 is an acceptance contradiction between the r8 AD22 refusal layer and ticket 03 (introduced by the r8/F1 repair), C5 is a dependency/ownership deadlock in the r8 task schedule (aggravated by the r8/F4 split).

| Finding | Class | Resolution in r9 |
|-------|-------|------------------|
| C4 — Client-token refusal weakens the persistence contract without owner approval: refusing persistence for every configured client token — even a clean, validated document containing only references — contradicts ticket `issues/03-last-known-good-config-fallback.md:14` ("A config that validates is persisted as the new last-known-good without rewriting the operator file"): such instances can never acquire a fallback and keep crash-looping after an invalid edit; AD20 approves only T2's evidence lane, not this loss of resilience | HIGH (statement-contradiction / ac-not-falsifiable) | **Closed — failure class resolved; R7 unchanged (enforced, not weakened); the ticket-03 persistence contract is RESTORED.** Owner decision recorded: `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/owner-envelope-widening-3.json` (recordedAt 2026-11-22T06:05:00Z, origin `user`, explicit, decision `approve-envelope-widening-3`, answer-field absent — scope text explicit) authorizes an exported resolver of resolved client-token values so clean client-token configurations can persist a secret-scanned last-known-good copy; canonical T3 write set += `internal/config/config.go`, `internal/config/config_test.go` (the ONLY set change at r9). **AD22 (rewritten): the refusal layer is REMOVED; the pre-persist gate is the single credential byte-containment scan over the exported resolvers' resolved values — per-server `ServerCredentials` (bearer + every header value), `EmbeddingCredential`, and the NEW exported `Config.ResolvedClientTokens() ([]string, error)` (internal/config/config.go, T3, widening 3), which resolves every configured `client_auth.bearer_tokens` entry through the same `resolveOne` reference resolver (credentials.go:154) at call time, mirroring `ServerCredentials` (credentials.go:42) / `EmbeddingCredential` (:141); a resolution error returns a metadata-only error (never a value) and contributes no scan input — unreachable for a validated candidate, whose every reference resolves (`reference_unresolved`); only resolved, non-empty values are scanned. No change to `ClientTokenHashes()`, the digest-only resolution pass, validation, or any other `internal/config` semantics.** Fixtures: AC8/AC10 gain the positive **`TestLastKnownGoodPersistsCleanClientTokenConfig`** — a valid document with `client_auth.bearer_tokens` configured (`{env: TOKEN}`), reference-only, no secret material anywhere in its bytes ⇒ the copy IS written (byte-identical to the validated bytes, atomic, 0600), exactly one `config_loaded`, service continues — proving the ticket-03 contract restored; and the negative **`TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnClientTokenInComment`** — the same config shape whose YAML comment carries the env-resolved client token value ⇒ NO copy written, exactly one sanitized `config_last_good_write_failed` warn record (code `last_good_write_failed`, `resource.path` = the copy path) naming the scan-skip class, `config_loaded` still emitted, service continues. The r8 refusal fixture `TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnClientTokenConfigured` is REMOVED WITH the refusal layer — superseded, not weakened: the scan enforces strictly more secret material than the refusal detected (a refusal keyed on configuration, never on values), and the removed fixture's observable (no copy + typed warn + service continues) is preserved by the new negative on the value-match path. `config_last_good_write_failed` covers TWO classes since r9 (persist I/O failure, credential-scan skip — a client-token match IS a scan skip); §4.4/§7/§8 class lists and the §10 prohibited-scope/non-goal follow-up wording are updated; the r8 non-goal "an exported `internal/config` client-token resolver (owner-gated follow-up)" is WITHDRAWN — the resolver is now the design under widening 3 |
| C5 — T3 cannot reach GREEN before its dependent T4: AC6(c)/AC8 require `config_loaded`/degraded emission while the emission calls belong exclusively to T4 (blockedBy T3) — the literal schedule deadlocks or forces an ownership violation/test weakening | HIGH (lifecycle-hazard) | **Closed — original fixture repaired; the schedule is acyclic; every final assertion preserved.** Final assertion ownership (r9, stated in §4.3/§6 and mirrored in §10): **T3 owns the ENTIRE `ReloadMetrics` hook body in `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve.go`** — the slot clear on every `rejected` report, the AD22-gated persist invocation on `success` (its own `last_known_good.go` helper), AND the `config_loaded`/`config_degraded` emission calls (AD5 ordering: loaded first, degraded second on a degraded source) — plus ALL THREE writers in `cmd/mcp-bridge/diagnostics_envelope.go` (`writeConfigLoaded`, the degraded writer, the last-good-write-failure writer) and the `ResolvedClientTokens()` resolver in `internal/config/config.go`; every emitting code path sits on files already in T3's set, so no set change is needed for the emission half (only widening 3 for the resolver). **T3's RED set gains `TestDegradedStartOrderLoadedThenDegraded`** (the AC6(c)/AD5 degraded emission-ordering fixture, landing in T3's `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve_edge_test.go`) and the C4 fixtures, plus the resolver unit tests in `internal/config/config_test.go` (`TestResolvedClientTokens*`); **every T3 assertion is reachable GREEN with T4 unstarted**: strict parsing is unset in every T3 fixture (the default keeps fallback behavior), T3's fixtures never assert strict-mode behavior, and T3's validation commands run no T4-owned test. **T4 CONSUMES the landed hook**: its implementation deliverable is the `MCP_STRICT_CONFIG` strict-decision insert in the `load` closure (before the fallback/copy read, AD4/I5) plus the operator docs; its assertion deliverables are the strict/default/degraded envelope matrix (AC12/AC13/AC16), the AC14 `TestConfigLoaded*` family, the E1 case and the normal inter-load consumption fixtures — permanent regression coverage that is GREEN ON ARRIVAL (T3's landed hook already emits; they are not RED fixtures and must not be weakened), the `TestStrictConfig*` family (RED: fallback exists pre-insert), and the G2 contract oracle + example/readme doc tests (RED: the doc sections do not exist today). `blockedBy T3` is UNCHANGED (canonical); **at r13/K2 T3's own `blockedBy` is canonically `["T1"]` (applied in `plan.json`, carried into the machine state by the r13 projection — landed and verified at r14)** — T3 depends on no implementation task besides T1; the dependency graph T0 → T1 → {T2 blockedBy T1, T3 blockedBy T1, T4 blockedBy T3} is acyclic. T4's fixture expectations require nothing of T3 beyond consuming its landed behavior: T3's RED/validation set contains no T4-authored test, and T4's set contains no test whose GREEN requires T3 to test strict behavior. No acceptanceId changes (AC12–AC16 remain T4's; AC6–AC11 remain T3's; AC14's fixtures are T4-owned consumption assertions over T3's landed emission) |

|-------|-------|------------------|

Consequence: every T3 effect path sits inside an authorized root — `effectCheck` (`inside()` against the writePaths roots, `execution-contract-binding.mjs:309-316`) passes for T3 and every task; the r9 "bound and USED at r9" claim is corrected throughout this spec to "approved at r9, **applied at r10 at both levels**"; §10 states the write roots and mirrors the per-task sets, and no claim remains that would fail `effectCheck`. (r11 status: the transition LANDED — the canonical state read at r11 was at revision r10 with the `internal/config` root in `writePaths` (§3); the r11 exact-file scope supersedes the root wording — see the r11 table. r12 status: the r11 projection LANDED too — the canonical state carried the TWO EXACT FILES (`execution-state.json:138-139`), verified at r12 and by CR11's own verification; see the r12 table J3. r13 status: the state advanced to revision r12 with the CR12 review transition (§3, r13/K3). r14 status (L2 currency): the state is at revision r13 with the T3 `blockedBy: ["T1"]` binding LANDED (verified at r14) — no re-apply step exists; the exact-file writePaths and T3 grant anchors are stable across the transitions.)** |
| H2 — The emission lane used raw `observe.WriteJSONLine` concurrently with the refresher's serialized `observe.Logger` writes with no shared serialization: the Logger mutex covers only its own writes (`internal/observe/log.go:31-36`; `Logger.write` at :121-124 locks `l.mu` around `output.Write`), while `WriteJSONLine` is an unlocked package function (`log.go:47-66`); with the emission lane on the `Loop.Start`/`watch` goroutine and refresh logs on the Refresher goroutine (§7), shared-buffer `-race` fixtures could race and production lines could interleave | MEDIUM (design-gap) | **Closed — failure class resolved; the emission lane is serialized.** AD12/§4.4/§7 amended (r10): `config_loaded`, `config_degraded`, `config_last_good_write_failed` — including every persist-path diagnostic — are emitted through **one shared cmd-owned locking writer pair already in the packet**: a `type serializedSink`-style `io.Writer` wrapper (mutex + inner writer, `Write` under the lock) declared in T3's `cmd/mcp-bridge/diagnostics_envelope.go` (in T3's set since r1 — no new path); serve constructs the pair once over the process sinks and constructs the `observe.Logger` over the SAME pair (`observe.NewLogger(stdout, stderr)` today at serve.go:108 — T3's file — becomes `observe.NewLogger(lockedOut, lockedErr)`), and the three cmd envelope writers marshal via `observe.WriteJSONLine` **into the pair**, never into the bare process sinks. The refresher/HTTP Logger path (`Logger.write`: `l.mu` → `output.Write` → the pair's `Write` under the pair mutex) and the emission path (`WriteJSONLine(pair)` → the pair's `Write` under the pair mutex) serialize on the pair's mutex at the byte level; lock order is strictly one-directional (`l.mu` → pair mutex; nothing acquires `l.mu` while holding the pair mutex) — no deadlock. **The literal alternative — emitting through the existing `observe.Logger` instance so its own mutex serializes the writes — is impossible without an `internal/observe` change:** `Logger`'s only exported write surface is `Log(Entry)` (log.go:85), whose `buildEnvelope`/`eventMessage`/`Fields`-allowlist Entry lane is closed (rejected alternative (e)); `internal/observe/log.go` is in NO task write set and is not added at r10 — T3 passes the sinks (the pair) to the Logger instead, achieving the same serialization with zero `internal/observe` change. New permanent fixture **`TestLastKnownGoodEmissionSerializedDuringRefreshPass`** (T3's RED set; matched by T3 validation command 1's existing `TestLastKnownGood` prefix — no command change): shared `bytes.Buffer` sinks wrapped in the pair, the Logger built over the pair, a refresh pass logging through the Logger concurrent with a `success` reload-report emission; asserts complete, newline-terminated, uninterleaved JSON lines and exactly one `config_loaded`, under `-race`. | H3 — R6 requires documenting all new events, but AC15's oracle asserted only `config_loaded`/`config_degraded`; a missing `config_last_good_write_failed` contract row would pass | LOW (ac-not-falsifiable) | **Closed — original fixture repaired.** AC15's observable (§5) and the G2 oracle description now require ALL THREE rows: `TestLoggingContractDocumentsConfigEventsAndStorageBase` (T4's set) asserts the contract doc carries the `config_loaded`, `config_degraded` AND `config_last_good_write_failed` event rows plus the storage-base statement and the document-level `config.property_path` `"<document>"` attribution wording — a missing third row fails the oracle. R6's text enumerates the three events explicitly (§2, clarification; no id change). The r6 G2 row's two-row wording is annotated as superseded by the three-row requirement |
| H4 — AD21 called the F2 gate boolean "already memoized" while the fixtures require it true only on the loop's second pre-publication call; the memo is never cleared, so a literal `memo != nil` is also true on the post-publication SIGHUP that AC6(c)/AC14 expect to be guard-rejected, hanging the fixture | LOW (statement-contradiction) | **Closed — original fixture repaired; the predicate is falsifiable.** AD21/§4.3 amended (r10): the load-gate hook's boolean is **`memo != nil && holder.publishedSettings() == nil`** — true exactly on the loop's second pre-publication load (the bootstrap call at serve.go:139 sees `memo == nil` → false → performs the load and stores the memo; `Loop.Start`'s call at loop.go:137 sees `memo != nil` and `publishedSettings() == nil` → true → the gate can hold it), and false on every post-publication load (`publishedSettings() != nil`) — so the post-publication SIGHUP reload proceeds down the normal reload path and is guard-rejected by the restart-only rule when B changes `storage.directory` (`restart_required`, F3) rather than hung on the gate. The memoized-return condition in §4.3 carries the same conjunction; the memo itself remains never-cleared (r7 design unchanged). The r8 F2 row's "already memoized" phrasing is superseded by this formula (annotated in the preserved r8 table; the row itself is a historical record). ### Review closure (r11 → CT10) — preserved from r11

Ledger: the CT10 critic review (verdict **REVISE**, findings C6, H2 (reopened), C7; coordinator-persisted at `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/review-crit-r10.txt`, recorded review `ReviewConfigAdmissionSpecCT10`); severity per the authoritative ledger text. Classes per `workflow-policy/review-findings-taxonomy.md`. Causality: all three are existing defects of the r10 spec carried from earlier revisions, not repair-induced regressions of landed code — C6 is the acceptance contradiction introduced by the r3/AD17 scope decision (startup admission carved out of R1's universal with no owner exception recorded), carried unchanged through r10; H2's reopen is the surviving half of the same existing design gap the r10 H2 repair addressed (the `operatorLogf` lane was never serialized — an r5 emission-design gap; the r10 repair neither caused nor fully closed it); C7 is an existing falsifiability defect of the T3 RED step as written since r1/r5 (baseline-preservation fixtures mislisted as required-RED), surfaced by CT10. The advisory CR10 code review (verdict **APPROVE**) is addressed alongside: its envelope item (directory root vs the owner's two-file delta) = the r11 exact-file scope (§10); its operatorLogf item = H2; its R1/AC1/AC5 item = C6; its AC5 second-oracle item = the `TestStartupCleanStartEmitsNoFailure` naming in AC5's command.

| Finding | Class | Resolution in r11 |
|-------|-------|------------------|
| C6 — Known admission failures still violate ticket 01's diagnostic contract: R1/§4.1 (r10 `spec.md:125`, §4.1 admission paragraph) requires every non-diagnostic exit to name its failing stage, but §4.1/AC5 mandated `unclassified`/`internal_error` for the known `AdmitStartup` failure path (`internal/service/loop.go:157-160`) — the class most likely behind a `startup_failed`/`internal_error` loop — with no owner scope exception recorded | HIGH (statement-contradiction / ac-not-falsifiable) | **Closed — original fixture repaired; failure class resolved; R1's universal restored to genuinely universal; no `internal/service` change, no owner exception, no AC weakening.** Root invariant: EVERY non-diagnostic serve exit names its failing stage — including the one runtime path this feature exists to diagnose. Repair across clauses (AD23, §4.1/§4.3/§5/§6): **(1) Vocabulary** — `admission` joins the closed startup-stage vocabulary (§4.1), still disjoint from the HTTP lane's `legacyStageVocabulary` (verified: httpdiagnostics.go:631-638 contains `host_admission`/`session_admission`/`tool_admission` compounds but NOT `admission`); the admission record rides the EXISTING `startup_failed` event family — no new event name; `stage` already exists in the envelope union, so NO count-pin/`EnvelopeFieldNames` change occurs at r11. **(2) T1 defines the classifier hook (startup_failure.go is T1's)** — `admissionStartupFailure()`: event `startup_failed`, operation `startup`, level `error`, outcome `failure`, code `admission_failed`, kind `configuration`, retryable true, static package-owned cause + remediation; and the START-EXIT CLASSIFIER ENTRY `startupFailureForStartExit(err error, admissionReported bool) startupFailure` with precedence: `errors.Is(service.ErrPublicationFailed)` → `publication`; `errors.Is(errCatalogLoopDidNotDrain)` → `catalog_loop`; `admissionReported` → `admissionStartupFailure()`; else `fallbackStartupFailure()` (`unclassified`/`internal_error`) — **(r13/K1) precedence amended: publication-sentinel → catalog-sentinel → admission-capture → typed-listener (`errors.As`) → `unclassified`/`internal_error`**. `startupFailureForError(err)` KEEPS its signature and delegates to `startupFailureForStartExit(err, false)` (its existing call sites compile unchanged: serve.go:409 until T3 swaps it, serve_edge_test.go:193). **(3) The discriminator is the EXISTING `("rejected","admission_failed")` reload report** (loop.go:159 fires it immediately before the startup admission error return; loop.go:110 fires it for reloads): T3's `ReloadMetrics` hook body (already T3's per r9/C5) additionally sets a synchronized cmd-owned capture boolean on `("rejected","admission_failed")`, and the drain tail classifies through the entry under a **`fromStartExit` gate** — true only when the classified failure IS the `loopErr` Start exit, false on every listenerErr/sentinel/ctx path — so the tail calls `startupFailureForStartExit(failure, fromStartExit && captured)`. **Exclusion evidence (verified, §3):** `Loop.Start`'s non-nil returns (load wrap :141, admission :157-160, publication wrap :164-166) all occur strictly BEFORE the `watch`/refresher drivers start (:180/:195), so when `fromStartExit` is true no `Reload` has ever run and the capture can only reflect the STARTUP admission report at :159; ## 2. REQUIREMENTS
<!-- specdd:requirements:start -->

**R1 (ticket 01) — typed, sanitized startup cause.** Every `serve` exit path that is not a config validation diagnostic MUST emit a `startup_failed`/`startup_publication_failed`-family record carrying (a) `error.code` naming the failing stage's class, (b) a new top-level `stage` identifier from the closed startup-stage vocabulary (§4.1), and (c) a new top-level `cause` summary that is package-owned static text plus already-envelope-safe identifiers only — never raw runtime error text. **This includes the `startup_publication_failed` family: that record carries stage `publication` and a static cause, verified by AC1 (E6).** **(r11/C6) This includes the STARTUP ADMISSION path: a `Loop.Start` error delivered on `loopErr` after a reported startup admission failure (the existing `("rejected","admission_failed")` reload report, loop.go:157-160) MUST classify stage `admission` with `error.code` `admission_failed` and a static package-owned cause (AD23) — no `internal/service` change, no owner exception.** **(r13/K1) This includes the LISTENER RUNTIME path, and it MUST be reachable in the classifier: `serveListener` wraps its `server.Serve` error in a cmd-owned typed error carrying the listener name and configured address, and `startupFailureForStartExit(err, admissionReported)` classifies it through an `errors.As` typed-listener branch BEFORE the unclassified fallback — stage `listener`, code `listener_runtime` (`listenerRuntimeFailure`) — so NO listener failure that ends `serve` classifies `unclassified`/`internal_error`.** **(r14/L1) The listener-runtime PRODUCER and the classifier entry are falsifiable by the one named oracle: `TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass` drives `serveListener` with a fake `net.Listener` whose `Accept` returns a deterministic non-temporary error, asserts the returned error is the cmd-owned wrapper carrying the listener name (`inbound`/`operations`) and the configured address, and asserts `startupFailureForStartExit` maps it to stage `listener`/code `listener_runtime` — removing the wrapper FAILS the test.** The paths that today collapse to `internal_error`/"no specific class matched" — the snapshot-quarantine-recovery raw error return, the catalog-loop conditions (the `service.NewLoop` construction failure, serve.go:282-315, and the drain-budget sentinel, the inline literal at serve.go:397) — gain their named stage/code/cause; the listener runtime path gains its typed classification (r13/K1: typed error + `errors.As` branch, §4.1; r14/L1: producer oracle, §4.1/§5); everything else genuinely unmatched — a start-exit error with NO captured admission report and no typed listener wrapper, and every unknown error — stays `unclassified`/`internal_error`. Load diagnostics keep the existing `*config.Diagnostics` path unchanged.

**R2 (ticket 02) — live diagnosis and code fix of the `mcp-bridge-network` crash loop.** Ticket 02's literal item 1 is preserved: the failing stage and cause MUST be "recorded from the live instance, quoting the typed diagnostic produced by ticket 01" (`.scratch/config-admission-resilience/issues/02-diagnose-and-fix-network-startup-loop.md:9`). Recorded conflict, now OWNER-RESOLVED: the deployed instance runs the pre-T1 build — its only startup diagnostic is the generic `startup_failed`/`internal_error` line — and no live effect is granted to this packet (AD18), so the typed pre-fix line cannot be quoted *from the live instance*. **The owner has APPROVED the evidence lane (AD20; decision artifact `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/owner-ticket02-evidence.json`, recorded 2026-11-22, explicit user decision, answer `approve`): the pre-fix typed stage/cause is captured by running the **post-T3 build** (T1's classifier entry + T3's wired admission capture, AD23 — landed before T2 per the r17/C9 order T1 → T3 → T2 → T4) against the live instance's actual config and state layout (owner-lane-supplied copy, not a reduced reproduction), BEFORE the fix is applied (AC17), while the ticket also records the live instance's literal generic line and container/`RestartCount` evidence verbatim as owner-supplied material. No T2 deploy/publish/network effect is granted by the approval.** The packet's **delivered remedy is a code fix inside this write envelope (§10)**, covered by the regression test `TestNetworkStartupRecoveryClass` in `cmd/mcp-bridge/network_startup_recovery_test.go`, which MUST fail as an assertion failure on the pre-fix code. The second bridge instance MUST reach `running`, pass its healthcheck, and serve one successful inbound MCP request, with `RestartCount` stable across at least ten minutes of observation — **live verification is owner/operator-lane evidence bound by the coordinator** (AC18, AD18). If the diagnosis concludes the fault is configuration-only (no code defect), no code remedy exists to land: T2 records the exact file/key/value verbatim in the ticket as an owner-gated operator follow-up (AD14) and reports its remedy step blocked-on-owner; that branch is evidence, not a packet remedy, and closes through the owner-applied follow-up plus AC18 (E4).

**R3 (ticket 03) — last-known-good fallback.** When the active config document is READ successfully but fails parse or semantic validation on a startup load (no published generation), and a persisted last-known-good copy exists and itself parses and validates, the process MUST start from that copy and serve, emitting exactly one `config_degraded` record (level `warn`, operation `load_config`, outcome `degraded`) naming the active config path, the class of the rejected value, and that value's line/property when the diagnostic locates a value — or the document-level key (`Key "<document>"`, property `document`, no line; emitted on the envelope as `config.property_path` `"<document>"` via the verbatim `PropertyPath = record.Key` mapping — AD15 r6/G4) for a document-level diagnostic such as `config_too_large`. **On the degraded branch the `load` closure MUST hand its caller the copy's effective candidate (returned via the unchanged `Load` signature) and the copy's effective settings (reaching the caller through the staged holder — `holder.stage`/`holder.settings()`), and the startup admission MUST admit that effective (copy) candidate — never a nil active-parse result (G1, wording corrected at r8 per F8).** The fallback MUST trigger only on validation diagnostics of a successfully read document; a read failure (`config_read`) fails closed with the active document's own diagnostics, exactly as today (AD13), and the cmd read-error diagnostics MUST be field-for-field identical to `config.ParseFile`'s (AD16). Reloads never fall back (I4). The copy is always re-read and re-validated at load, never trusted (AD3); the operator's active file is never rewritten (I3).

**R4 (ticket 04) — strict mode.** `MCP_STRICT_CONFIG=true` MUST disable the last-known-good fallback entirely: an invalid active config exits with the existing startup failure even when a validated copy exists, and no `config_degraded` is emitted. The default (unset or non-true) keeps R3 behavior unchanged.

**R5 (ticket 04) — `config_loaded`, one bootstrap generation, persist safety.** Every accepted config generation MUST emit exactly one `config_loaded` record (level `info`, operation `load_config`, outcome `success`) **from the cmd-owned reload-report emission lane (`LoopOptions.ReloadMetrics` observing outcome `success`, serve.go:306), which fires exactly once per accepted generation — after admission and after the publication chain returned nil — and never for a refresh pass, a rejected load, or a failed publication (AD12, r5)**: once per process start (normal or degraded; a degraded start's record carries the copy path) and once per admitted reload. A load rejected before admission (restart-only guard, runtime admission) MUST emit no `config_loaded` and MUST NOT overwrite the last-known-good copy. A refresh pass MUST NOT emit `config_loaded` or persist anything — its publications ride the same `Published` closure (loop.go:81, refresh.go:455), which this design leaves byte-identical and emission-free, so refresh-originated publications bypass the emission lane entirely. A publication (projection) failure MUST emit `startup_publication_failed` (stage `publication`) and NO `config_loaded` — the failure return precedes the success report, so its outcome is never consumed. **Publication-emission invariant (r5, E1): an emission or persistence is reachable only through a `success` reload report of the load that staged the pending outcome — a rejected admission can never be followed by an emission or a persistence, for identical or changed content alike (§4.3, AC14).** **Bootstrap-generation invariant (r7, C1/AD21/I13): the startup bootstrap load (serve.go:139) and the loop's own startup load (loop.go:137) MUST admit, publish, persist and report ONE generation — the bootstrap snapshot — which is the same generation that configures storage, listeners, diagnostics and metrics; an inter-load A→B edit MUST NOT be admitted, persisted or reported while the resources use A (AC14/AC6(c) fixtures, deterministic since r8 via the F2 test-gate seam, whose boolean is `memo != nil && publishedSettings() == nil` at r10/H4).** **Persist safety (r7 C2 + r9 C4/AD22): the persisted bytes MUST pass the pre-persist credential scan before any write — the scan covers every resolved secret value the document's own references resolve to — per-server `ServerCredentials` bearer + header values, `EmbeddingCredential`, the resolved client-token values via the exported `Config.ResolvedClientTokens()` resolver (r9/widening 3), AND the `tool_metadata` nested `{env:}`/`{file:}` reference values, all enumerated since r15/C8 by the exported complete-inventory `Config.ResolvedSecrets()` accessor (owner secret-inventory decision; §3/AD22); on any match the persistence is skipped and exactly one sanitized `config_last_good_write_failed` record is emitted (AC8 negatives). A clean, validated client-token configuration (reference-only, no resolved value anywhere in its bytes) persists its copy exactly like any other validated config — the ticket-03 contract (issues/03:14) is NOT weakened (r9/C4: the r8 refusal layer is removed).**

**R6 (ticket 04) — documented storage base, within T4's owned files.** `configs/config.example.yaml`, `README.md`, and `docs/logging-contract.md` MUST state the one resolution base for a relative `storage.directory` (the config file's own directory — current, deliberately tested behavior; §3). `README.md` carries no `storage.directory` documentation today (verified at r3); T4 adds that section. The structured-logging contract MUST document the new events — **all three: `config_loaded`, `config_degraded`, AND `config_last_good_write_failed` (r10/H3)** — and the new envelope fields (`stage`, `cause`). **The contract's `config_loaded`, `config_degraded` and `config_last_good_write_failed` rows and the storage-base statement are enforced by the named permanent test `TestLoggingContractDocumentsConfigEventsAndStorageBase` in `cmd/mcp-bridge/logging_contract_test.go`, owned by T4 under the r6 canonical amendment (AC15, G2; the three-row requirement added at r10/H3).** **Out of scope, recorded not claimed (E2): `Dockerfile:26-28` documents a wrong operator-facing base (process working directory → `/app/data`) that contradicts the tested config-file-directory base; correcting it requires adding `Dockerfile` to T4's write set, which this packet does not do (no owner widening). That correction is a PENDING owner-gated follow-up (AD19); T4 returns it verbatim through its receipt and the coordinator records it in the ticket-04 record (C3) — R6's operator-docs clause is NOT claimed satisfied by this packet.** This packet MUST NOT add a validation rejecting a `storage.directory` that resolves inside the config file's own directory: deployed instances use that layout (`/app/configs/data`); that rejection is recorded as a documented follow-up (AD10).

**R7 — secret hygiene.** No secret value, bearer token, or credential may appear in any startup event, in the `config_degraded`/`config_loaded` records, or in the persisted fallback copy.

**R8 — compatibility and non-goals.** No new dependency; no change to breaker recovery, reload tolerance (MB-006), embedding budgets (MB-004), embedding-pass observability (MB-005), or the client-harness pause (MB-003); `--check-config` behavior unchanged (AD11); exit codes unchanged; `storage.Open`/`Parse` resolution semantics unchanged (AD7); no `internal/service` change; **the ONLY `internal/config` change is the owner-widening-3 pair (`internal/config/config.go`, `internal/config/config_test.go`, T3's set): the exported `Config.ResolvedClientTokens() ([]string, error)` resolver and its unit tests (r9/C4; anchors corrected at r10/H5), plus — r15/C8, owner secret-inventory decision, decision artifact `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/owner-secret-inventory-authorization.json` — the exported complete-inventory `Config.ResolvedSecrets() ([]string, error)` accessor and its unit tests in the SAME pair; no change to `ClientTokenHashes()` (config.go:81), the digest-only resolution pass (the append at :433), validation rules, `Parse`/`ParseFile`, `ServerCredentials`/`EmbeddingCredential`, the `tool_metadata`/diagnostics machinery (the `_meta` shape gate tool_metadata.go:140-148 permitting arbitrary nested mappings; the generic reference-resolution walk diagnostics.go:271-448, `resolveSecrets` ~:630-648, retained on `Config.resolved` — config.go:68,384,455 — read anchors), or any other `internal/config` semantics**.

**Non-goals.** Per-upstream optional servers and reload tolerance (MB-006); embedding request/pass budget split (MB-004); embedding pass events (MB-005); the Hermes client pause (MB-003); cached-session eviction; any new metric, dashboard, or alert; rejecting a state directory inside the config directory (documented follow-up, R6/AD10); changing `--check-config`; altering restart supervision policy; a typed `internal/service` admission-error sentinel (owner-gated follow-up, §4.1 — **not needed since r11: AD23 classifies startup admission cmd-side via the existing reload report; the follow-up remains recorded, unapproved, and unused**); **correcting the `Dockerfile:26-28` operator comment — owner-gated follow-up, PENDING owner decision, NOT approved, NOT in any task write set (E2/AD19)**; any deployed/host configuration change (owner-gated operator follow-up, R2/AD14); any T2 deploy, publish, network, SSH, registry-push, container-restart or inbound-live-probe effect (AD18 — live AC18 evidence is produced by the owner/operator lane); **a live pre-fix quote from the deployed instance — the owner-APPROVED lane (AD20) binds the pre-fix typed capture to running the **post-T3 build** locally (admission capture wired by T3, AD23 — landed before T2, r17/C9) against the instance's actual config/state layout; the approval grants no live effect (E3)**; a load-time secret scan of the fallback copy (not required: no copy can pre-date T3 and every persist is AD22-gated — §4.2); any write outside the per-task fences in §10. **WITHDRAWN at r9 (C4/widening 3): the r8 non-goal "an exported `internal/config` client-token resolver (owner-gated follow-up, r8/F1)" — the resolver is now the approved design under widening 3.**

(r14/L1) `TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass`'s extended producer assertions (fake-listener `Accept` failure through `serveListener`) reference the same pre-implementation symbols as the r13 entry assertions — the compile-RED carve-out reason is unchanged, and no new pre-implementation symbol is introduced beyond the r13 set.**

For the `config_too_large` document-level diagnostic (`Key: "<document>", Property: "document"`, config.go:99-104) the emitted `config.property_path` is therefore the literal **`"<document>"`**, and the diagnostic's `Property: "document"` value maps to **no envelope field** (ConfigInfo has no `Property` member — envelope.go:43). - **`internal/config/config.go:904-963` (re-read at r8 for F1)** — `validateScalarType`: `client_auth.bearer_tokens` must be mapping (reference) nodes or a sequence of them (`section == "client_auth" && key == "bearer_tokens"`, :927-933); an inline scalar value fails `reference_required` (:937-944). The client-token resolution pass resolves each configured entry to its value and stores only its sha256 digest in the unexported `clientTokenHashes` field (field at config.go:68; digest append verified in the pass around config.go:420-446).
- **`internal/config/config.go:81` (re-anchored at r10/H5)** — `func (c Config) ClientTokenHashes() [][32]byte` returns a copy of the digest slice and is **unchanged by this packet**. **`internal/config/config.go:433` (re-anchored at r10/H5)** — the digest-population line of the client-token resolution pass: `cfg.clientTokenHashes = append(cfg.clientTokenHashes, sha256.Sum256([]byte(value)))` (inside the pass range config.go:420-446); the pass stores only sha256 digests, never values. ### [AUTO-DECISION] entries (for coordinator persistence in the feature decisions ledger)

- **AD1 — cause is static package-owned text plus envelope-safe identifiers; raw error text is never copied.** Rationale: deterministic secret-safety (R7) and the existing contract ("arbitrary Go error text is never a log field"). The rejected alternative — a redacted, truncated `err.Error()` — would plumb resolved secret values into the failure-writing path. Consequence: ticket-02 diagnosis identifies the stage from `stage`/`error.code` plus the supplied live config/state evidence, not from echoed error prose. **(r11/C6) the admission record's cause is package-owned static text (AD23) like every other startup cause. (r13/K1) the listener-runtime record's resource fields are the listener name and configured address — envelope-safe identifiers from the typed wrapper, never raw `err.Error()` text; the wrapper itself carries no secret material. (r14/L1) the producer fixture's fake `Accept` error text never reaches any asserted field — the wrapper assertions check the name/address fields and the wrapper TYPE, and the classifier maps the typed wrapper, not the underlying error text.**
- **AD2 — the last-known-good copy is the raw document bytes — the exact slice the validating load parsed — persisted next to the active file at `<config-dir>/<basename>.last-good.yaml`** (sibling of the operator file; e.g. for `/app/configs/config-network.yaml` → **`/app/configs/config-network.yaml.last-good.yaml`** — filename corrected at r5 for E5 to follow the normative `<basename>.last-good.yaml` rule). Rationale (corrected at r7 for C2, at r8 for F1, and at r9 for C4): validation rejects inline credential values in credential FIELDS (config.go:809, :937-944) — it does NOT sanitize comments or noncredential text (fingerprint_test.go:83-91), so raw-byte safety is ENFORCED by the AD22 pre-persist credential scan (client tokens included since r9; the COMPLETE resolved-secret inventory — server, embedding, client-token AND `tool_metadata` nested `{env:}`/`{file:}` values — enumerated since r15/C8 by the exported `ResolvedSecrets()` accessor), not inferred from validation; `{file:}` refs and a relative `storage.directory` resolve against the config file's directory, so a sibling copy resolves identically; the path is derivable before any successful parse; basename-derived names keep multiple configs in one directory distinct.
- **AD3 — the copy is always re-read and re-validated at load; never trusted.** A corrupt, tampered, or stale-invalid copy is treated as no fallback: fail closed with the ACTIVE config's original diagnostics, never the copy's.
- **AD4 — strict parsing mirrors the existing env-flag pattern** (`strings.EqualFold(v,"true") || v=="1"`, cf. `sessionAdmissionEnabled` serve.go:34-36). Any other value (unset, "false", "yes", anything) keeps the default: fallback enabled.
- **AD5 — on a degraded start, `config_loaded` precedes `config_degraded`**: the loaded record states what is served; the degraded record states what was rejected. Both run only from the emission lane, after successful publication (AD12 r5). **The emission calls making this ordering real are T3's since r9 (C5) — T3's hook body emits both records; T4 consumes them.**
- **AD6 — last-known-good persistence happens once per accepted generation sourced from a validated active document** (process start and each admitted reload), atomically (temp file in the same directory, fsync, rename; mode 0600), best-effort, from the exact validated bytes (no persistence-time re-read), driven from the emission lane after the `success` report (AD12 r5) **and gated by the AD22 pre-persist credential scan (r7; client-token values included since r9/C4; the COMPLETE resolved-secret inventory — server, embedding, client-token AND `tool_metadata` nested `{env:}`/`{file:}` values — enumerated since r15/C8 by the exported `ResolvedSecrets()` accessor; the r8 refusal layer is removed): any resolved-secret match skips the write and emits one sanitized `config_last_good_write_failed` warn record instead — a skip is not a failure**. A write failure emits one `config_last_good_write_failed` warn record per failed attempt and NEVER fails startup or the reload — a read-only config directory must not recreate the MB-007 crash loop. A load rejected before admission persists nothing.
- **AD7 — `config_loaded` reports `filepath.Abs(settings.StorageDirectory)` at emission only.** `Parse`/`storage.Open` resolution semantics are untouched; only the event carries the absolute form.
- **AD8 — new top-level envelope fields `stage` and `cause` (JSON `stage`/`cause`, omitempty) on the shared `observe.Envelope`; the startup-stage vocabulary is closed, enforced by a membership test (AC1), and uses `unclassified` — not `unknown` — for the unmatched class, disjoint from the HTTP lane's legacy stage vocabulary (httpdiagnostics.go:631-638).** `stage` already exists in the documented field union (HTTP lane); `cause` joins `envelopeFieldNames`, `docs/logging-contract.md`, the `log_test.go:46-79` union literal, and the `config_envelope_test.go:166-170` count pin (130→131) — all inside T1's write set (the two test paths via the owner-approved r3/r4 widenings). **(r11/C6) the vocabulary gains the member `admission` (AD23) — verified disjoint from the HTTP lane's values; no field-union/count change.**
- **AD9 — no persistence on a degraded start** (the active document did not validate); the stored copy remains the previous validated one.
- **AD10 — no inside-config-dir storage rejection in this packet** (hard constraint; the deployed healthy instance uses `/app/configs/data`). MB-009's rejection acceptance item is a documented follow-up for a separate owner decision.
- **AD11 — `--check-config` unchanged**: no persistence, no fallback on the check path; it validates the ACTIVE file only. **The AD21 bootstrap memo lives inside the serve `load` closure and is not consulted on the check path; the F2 test gate lives in the same closure and is never armed on the check path (which does not enter it). (r11/C6) AD23's capture lives in the serve `ReloadMetrics` hook and the drain tail — the check path constructs neither. (r13/K1) the typed `serveListener` wrapper lives on the serve listener path only — the check path binds no listener. (r14/L1) the producer fixture drives `serveListener` directly in the cmd test surface — the check path is not entered and no check-path behavior is asserted or changed by it.**
- **AD12 (REDESIGNED at r5 for E1; slot payload corrected at r8/F9; ownership reconciled at r9/C5; write serialization added at r10/H2; operatorLogf routing added at r11/H2 (AD24(a)) — supersedes the r4 fingerprint-keyed pending-admission cell and the r8/F4 persist-vs-emit split) — emission and persistence ride the cmd-owned reload-report hook, consuming a plain pending load-outcome slot; NEVER a publication callback.** Rationale: the `Published` closure rides admissions AND every successful refresh pass (loop.go:81, refresh.go:455); for an unchanged-content reload the staged fingerprint equals the published generation's, so a refresh publication after a rejected admission matched the r4 cell and emitted (E1). No in-band field distinguishes those publications (identical fingerprint; generation advances on every build mode, reload.go:524-527), and cmd cannot see the admission call stacks (`watch` invokes `l.Reload()` internally, loop.go:204) — the holder is no discriminator either (`publishObserver` commits on every publication, wiring.go:394-397). The one cmd-owned synchronous signal that fires exactly once per accepted generation — after admission AND after the publication chain returns nil — and never for a refresh pass or a rejected load is `LoopOptions.ReloadMetrics` outcome `success` (serve.go:306; fired at loop.go:122 and :168 only; call-site set verified, §3). Consequences: (1) `load` stages a plain pending load-outcome (single mutex-guarded cmd-owned slot: **effective source (active-with-`data` or copy/degraded), the exact validated active bytes, the resolved settings, the degraded attribution or none, and — r7 — the resolved credential values, the AD22 gate inputs, resolved once in `load`; the slot never serializes them into any event** — the r7 "candidate fingerprint for the record" payload is REMOVED at r8/F9: no event or gate consumes a fingerprint, and `Assembly.Generation()`/`ConfigFingerprint()` (reload.go:117,121) remain read-only record fields) — loads are serialized (the startup loads precede the drivers, loop.go:168→172→180/:195; every reload load runs on the single `watch` goroutine), so one slot suffices and the newest load overwrites; (2) the `Published` closure (serve.go:307-309) stays byte-identical and never emits, persists, or touches the slot — **refresh-originated publications bypass the outcome lane entirely**; (3) the report hook consumes the slot on `success` — **emitting `config_loaded`, then `config_degraded` on a degraded source per AD5, and invoking the AD22-gated persist of a validated active source: the ENTIRE hook body (persist/clear AND the emission calls, and — r11/C6 — the AD23 admission-capture set on `("rejected","admission_failed")`) is T3's per r9/C5; T4 consumes the landed hook and writes none of it** — and **clears the slot on every `rejected` report**; (4) a publication failure returns before the success report (loop.go:117-118/:162-163), so its slot is never consumed — a stale slot is always overwritten by the next load before any later success report; (5) therefore **a rejected admission can never be followed by an emission or a persistence — for identical or changed content alike** (the publication-emission invariant, §4.3/R5, tested by AC14's `TestRejectedReloadThenRefreshPublicationEmitsNothing`). **(r10/H2) WRITE SERIALIZATION: every emission-lane and persist-path write — `config_loaded`, `config_degraded`, `config_last_good_write_failed` (persist I/O failure and AD22 scan skip alike) — goes through ONE shared cmd-owned locking writer pair (a `serializedSink`-style `io.Writer` wrapper in T3's `cmd/mcp-bridge/diagnostics_envelope.go`, no new path), and serve constructs the refresher/HTTP `observe.Logger` over the SAME pair (`observe.NewLogger(lockedOut, lockedErr)`, serve.go:108 — T3's file), so the Logger path (`l.mu` → pair mutex) and the emission path (`WriteJSONLine(pair)` → pair mutex) are mutually serialized at the byte level; lock order is one-directional (`l.mu` → pair mutex), no deadlock; `internal/observe/log.go` is NOT modified and NOT added to any write set (it is in no task set) — `Logger`'s only exported write method is `Log(Entry)` (log.go:85), so the literal route through the Logger instance is impossible without an `internal/observe` change (rejected alternative (e)); the exit/validation writers (`startup_failure.go:130`, `main.go:41/45/58/60` — the raw `writeStartupFailure` callers, r12/J4 (`main.go:83` is inside `checkConfig`, a stdout `WriteJSONLine`, not a startup-failure writer), `diagnostics_envelope.go:12`, `wiring.go:519`) stay on their raw sinks — they run on sequential exit paths outside the concurrent refresh window, with the r11 residuals disclosed at AD24(b)/(c). Enforced by `TestLastKnownGoodEmissionSerializedDuringRefreshPass` (T3's RED set, `-race`). (r11/H2) the `operatorLogf` lane joins the same serialization via AD24(a) — enforced by `TestOperatorLogfSerializedDuringRefreshPass`.**
- **AD13 — the fallback triggers only on validation diagnostics of a successfully read active document, document-level diagnostics included.** A read failure (`config_read`: not_found, permission_denied, io_error — `Key "<document>"`, no line) fails closed with the active document's own read diagnostics, exactly as today, so `config_degraded` never carries an unattributable read error. A deleted or unreadable config file keeps today's exit-1 contract even when a copy exists.
- **AD14 — T2 lands code fixes only; deployed/host configuration changes are owner-gated operator follow-ups recorded verbatim in the ticket; the configuration-only branch is evidence, not a packet remedy (E4).** The envelope grants T2 no write to any deployed configuration; if the diagnosis demands one, T2 records it (exact file/key/value) and the fix step reports blocked-on-owner until the follow-up is applied by the owner lane, after which AC18's live verification runs in the owner lane.
- **AD15 — document-level attribution is defined, not the fallback restricted (r3 fix for C5; PropertyPath value corrected at r6 for G4).** `config_too_large` (config.go:97-104) is a readable-document validation diagnostic with `Key "<document>", Property: "document"`, and no line; a degraded start on it emits the single `config_degraded` with document-level attribution: active path, `error.code=config_too_large`, `config` = `ConfigInfo{Path, PropertyPath: "<document>", Line: 0 (omitted), Shape: "over_limit", DiagnosticsIncomplete, RemainingStages}`. **Explicit mapping (r6): the envelope writer assigns `ConfigInfo.PropertyPath = record.Key` verbatim (diagnostics_envelope.go:41-56); for this diagnostic `record.Key` is the literal `"<document>"` (config.go:99-104), so the emitted `config.property_path` is `"<document>"` — NOT `"document"` (the r3–r5 wording was a spec defect); the diagnostic's `Property: "document"` names the property class and maps to no envelope field.** AD15, AC6(b), the G2 contract assertion, and the emitted record agree on `"<document>"` — the same value today's `config_validation_failed` record emits for this diagnostic. Rationale: restricting the fallback to value-located diagnostics would fail a >1 MiB active config closed even with a valid copy — losing exactly the resilience ticket 03 demands.
- **AD16 — the cmd read-error path duplicates `ParseFile`'s `config_read` fields locally, and parity is enforced by test (r3 fix for C6).** The `load` closure's read error constructs `&config.Diagnostics{Records: []config.Diagnostic{{Path: path, Key: "<document>", Code: "config_read", Property: "document", Shape: shape, Reason: "cannot read configuration file"}, RemainingStages: "not_checked"}` (exported `Records`, diagnostics.go:153-167), with the same three-way `os.IsNotExist`/`os.IsPermission` shape mapping. Rationale: exporting a read-diagnostics helper from `internal/config` needs owner authorization not granted, and re-calling `ParseFile` would re-read the file (a second, unvalidated I/O race). **(r11/C7) `TestConfigReadErrorParityWithParseFile` is a GREEN-on-arrival compatibility fixture — identical by construction pre-T3 (serve delegates to `ParseFile`), enforced post-T3 against the local construction; never counted as RED (AD24(d)).**
- **AD17 — `catalog_loop` names the two cmd-owned catalog-loop conditions — the `service.NewLoop` construction failure (serve.go:282-315) and the drain-budget sentinel (the inline literal at serve.go:397; anchor corrected at r5 for E5) (r3 fix for C2/F3; scope corrected at r4 for D4; startup-admission scope superseded at r11/C6 by AD23).** The inline `errors.New("the catalog loop did not drain")` at serve.go:397 becomes `var errCatalogLoopDidNotDrain = errors.New("the catalog loop did not drain")` in `cmd/mcp-bridge`, matched by the stage-aware classifier with `errors.Is` (it may flow through `firstError`); the EXISTING `catalogLoopFailure()` return on the `service.NewLoop` error branch gains stage `catalog_loop` and a static cause. Rationale: both are cmd-owned failures of loop construction/drain deserving the name. **(r11/C6) the r3–r10 clause "admission errors must keep `unclassified`/`internal_error` (AC5)" is SUPERSEDED at the STARTUP exit: a `Loop.Start` admission error classifies `admission`/`admission_failed` via AD23's entry; reload-time admission rejections (loop.go:110) keep today's behavior — no startup record, watch logs via `operatorLogf` — unchanged; no `catalog_loop` label leaks onto any admission error.**
- **AD18 — T2 records local, file-based evidence only; live verification is an owner/operator-lane step (r4 fix for D2, preserved at r5).** The canonical envelope allows only read/write/test effects and forbids network/publish/deploy/schedule/credentials/infrastructure; T2's effects are exactly its four bound write paths. T2 consumes the owner-supplied instance evidence (the recorded container state, the stderr cycle lines, and a local copy of the instance's actual config/state layout), reproduces locally against that actual layout, lands the code fix and the regression test, and writes the ticket record. AC17's quoted typed line comes from that local reproduction — **the owner-APPROVED lane (AD20)**; AC18's live verification (redeploy, healthcheck, one successful inbound MCP request, ≥10-minute `RestartCount` observation) is produced by the owner/operator lane after delivery and bound into the ticket by the coordinator.
- **AD19 (NEW at r5 for E2; recording path corrected at r7 for C3; unchanged PENDING at r9–r15) — the `Dockerfile` operator-comment correction is an owner-gated follow-up, PENDING, not approved, not in this packet.** `Dockerfile:26-28` documents a wrong base (process working directory → `/app/data`) contradicting the tested config-file-directory base (config.go:380-381; live layout `/app/configs/data`). Correcting it requires adding `Dockerfile` to T4's write set; no owner widening is available (hard constraint: no `Dockerfile`). Consequence: R6/AC15 cover only the T4-owned operator files; the wrong operator statement persists until the owner decides. **T4 returns the follow-up verbatim through its task receipt; the COORDINATOR records it into the ticket-04 file in the coordinator's designated lane — T4 writes no path outside its fence (C3); ticket 04 is and remains in NO task write set.** Nothing in this packet claims that operator doc corrected.
- **AD20 (NEW at r5 for E3; PENDING→APPROVED at r7; unchanged APPROVED at r9–r15) — the ticket-02 pre-fix evidence lane is OWNER-APPROVED.** Decision artifact `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/owner-ticket02-evidence.json` (recordedAt 2026-11-22T05:10:00Z, origin `user`, explicit, answer **`approve`**, decision `approve-ticket02-evidence-lane`): the ticket's literal "recorded from the live instance, quoting the typed diagnostic" is satisfied for the PRE-FIX quote by running the **post-T3 build** against the live instance's ACTUAL config and state layout (owner-lane-supplied copy) before the fix — a local capture, not a live-instance quote (r17/C9: the post-T3 build, because the admission capture T2 must observe is wired by T3, AD23 — T1's classifier unit alone leaves the drain tail delegating `false`); the deployed pre-T1 build's only diagnostic is the generic `startup_failed`/`internal_error` line, and no live effect is granted (AD18). Post-fix live running/restart proof remains owner/operator evidence (AC18). **The approval grants NO T2 deploy/publish/network effect.** AC17 records the approved decision reference alongside the quoted local typed line and the live instance's literal generic line + `RestartCount` evidence verbatim as owner-supplied material. If the owner later revokes the decision, the pre-fix live quote becomes an owner-lane step exactly like AC18, with no packet effect change.
- **AD21 (NEW at r7 for C1; deterministic seam added at r8/F2; accepted at r9; gate boolean corrected at r10/H4) — one bootstrap generation end-to-end: the cmd-owned `load` closure memoizes the first successful startup load's effective snapshot while the holder's published settings remain nil; every subsequent `load` call in that window returns the memoized snapshot WITHOUT disk I/O.** The memo holds the effective candidate, settings, exact validated active bytes, source attribution, and the resolved credential values (AD22 inputs) — the same values the load-outcome slot stages (AD12). Rationale: serve's bootstrap call (serve.go:139) configures storage (:154), listeners (:317-324), diagnostics/metrics (:145-152) from the FIRST load, while `Loop.Start` (loop.go:137) loads again before the first publication — an inter-load A→B edit would otherwise be admitted/published/persisted/reported as B while the resources still use A (CT6 C1, root window verified in §3: exactly two loads pre-publication; `watch`/refresher start only after the startup publication). The memoized return makes the loop's admission, the publication, the outcome slot, the emission and the persistence use the SAME generation as the resources — cmd-only (the memo lives in the existing closure in serve.go, T3's set), no `internal/service` change (R8). The memoized call re-stages the identical holder state and outcome slot (idempotent — the I12/G1 effective-candidate discipline). Reloads (published non-nil at load entry) NEVER read the memo — they load from disk under the restart-only guard exactly as today (I4); `--check-config` is untouched (AD11); strict/read-error/fail-closed branches return before any memo exists. **Deterministic test seam (r8/F2, boolean corrected at r10/H4): a cmd-package-level test-only load-gate hook variable in `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve.go`, invoked at the entry of every `load` closure call with the boolean `memo != nil && holder.publishedSettings() == nil` — NOT a bare "already memoized" flag (the memo is never cleared, so a bare `memo != nil` would also be true on every post-publication load and would hang the AC6(c)/AC14 SIGHUP fixtures that expect the guard-rejected reload path; the conjoined predicate is true exactly on the loop's second pre-publication load — the bootstrap call sees no memo, performs the load, stores the memo; `Loop.Start`'s call sees both conditions true; every post-publication load sees non-nil published settings, so the gate is never armed for the reload path); nil hook in production — a no-op with zero production effect and no timing dependency; the fixture assigns the gate before starting serve/`Loop.Start`, the gate blocks the memoized (second) call on a channel until the fixture releases it after mutating the active file, and the fixture resets the hook to nil via `t.Cleanup` — hook lifetime is the test's lifetime only. The gate makes both inter-load fixtures deterministic (the mutation provably lands inside the window) and the memo falsifiable (a removed memo ⇒ the second load reads the disk and fail the oracles).** Alternatives rejected: (j) fingerprint-compare-and-fail-closed at the loop's load — it converts a benign mid-startup operator edit into a new startup failure class, adds a rejection semantic to the bootstrap path, and still needs the bytes re-read; (k) a `LoopOptions`/loop-side bootstrap-candidate field — an `internal/service` change, prohibited by R8; the cmd-owned closure memo achieves the same contract; (m, r8) a sleep/timing-based fixture holding the window — rejected (CR7 F2): non-deterministic, the mutation normally lands after loop.go:137 and both fixtures could pass with the memo removed — the explicit gate is the falsifiable seam.
- **AD22 (NEW at r7 for C2; refusal layer added at r8/F1 and REMOVED at r9/C4 — the ticket-03 persistence contract is restored; at r10/H2 the skip/failure records ride the serialized writer pair) — the persisted copy's secret safety is ENFORCED by a pre-persist credential byte-containment scan on the exact validated bytes; on any match the persistence is SKIPPED and one typed, sanitized `config_last_good_write_failed` record is emitted — R7 unchanged, and ticket 03's validated-config-persists contract (issues/03:14) holds for clean client-token configurations.** Single scan layer: before any persistence attempt, the exact validated active bytes are scanned (`bytes.Contains` over the whole raw slice — comments and arbitrary noncredential text covered by construction, fingerprint_test.go:83-91) for EVERY resolved secret value the document's own references resolve to — **enumerated since r15/C8 by the exported COMPLETE-INVENTORY accessor `Config.ResolvedSecrets() ([]string, error)` (internal/config/config.go, the widening-3 pair file; owner decision `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/owner-secret-inventory-authorization.json` — an IN-FILE extension of the widening-3 export grant, NO set change), which the scan iterates EXCLUSIVELY and which composes the unchanged per-class resolvers**: each configured server id's `ServerCredentials(serverID)` resolved `Bearer` and every resolved `Headers` value (credentials.go:42), `EmbeddingCredential()` (credentials.go:141), — r9/C4, owner widening 3 — each resolved client-token value via the exported `Config.ResolvedClientTokens() ([]string, error)` (which resolves every configured `client_auth.bearer_tokens` entry through the same `resolveOne` reference resolver, credentials.go:154, at call time, mirroring `ServerCredentials`/`EmbeddingCredential`), **and — r15/C8 — every `tool_metadata` value that is or nests a `{env:}`/`{file:}` secret reference, resolved to its value by the same generic reference walk (internal/config/diagnostics.go:271-448, `resolveSecrets` ~:630-648, retained on `Config.resolved` — config.go:68,384,455; the `_meta` shape gate internal/config/tool_metadata.go:140-148 permits the arbitrary nested mappings), a class the r7–r14 inventory missed entirely**; a resolution error returns a metadata-only error (never a value) and contributes no scan input — unreachable for a validated candidate, whose every configured reference resolves (`reference_unresolved` fails validation).** Only successfully resolved, NON-EMPTY values are scanned (an unresolvable or empty reference contributes no secret material; reference NAMES are not secrets). On any match: skip the persistence attempt entirely (no copy written, no rename) and emit exactly one `config_last_good_write_failed` warn record. **NO refusal layer: a configured `client_auth.bearer_tokens` alone no longer refuses persistence (r9/C4 — the r8 layer violated ticket `issues/03-last-known-good-config-fallback.md:14`; the owner accepted the scan-based path via `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/owner-envelope-widening-3.json`).** The record family covers the TWO skip/failure classes — persist I/O failure and credential-scan skip (a client-token match IS a scan skip) — same level (warn), operation (`load_config`), outcome (`failure`), `error.code=last_good_write_failed`, kind resource, `resource.path` = the copy path, static package-owned message naming which class; never the matched bytes, any credential reference, or any secret (sanitized like every cmd record — AD1); one record per skipped/failed attempt; the start/reload continues normally; `config_loaded`/`config_degraded` unchanged. **(r10/H2) The skip/failure records are written through the shared serialized writer pair (AD12 r10) — serialized against the refresher/HTTP Logger writes like every emission-lane record.** False positives (a short resolved value coincidentally appearing in valid text) are SAFE: they skip persistence, never leak, and are operator-visible via the warn record; the copy stays at the previous validated generation. The gate runs ONLY at the persist step (AD6, emission lane, after the `success` report); degraded starts never persist (AD9); no pre-fix copy can exist anywhere (persistence itself is introduced by T3 — an old binary writes no copies), so no load-time copy scan is required. **AC8's negative fixtures enforce the skip (`TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnSecretInComment`, `TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnSecretInNonCredentialText` (r7), `TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnClientTokenInComment` (r9), `TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnToolMetadataSecretInComment` (r15/C8 — a resolved `tool_metadata` reference value in a comment blocks persistence with the typed event)) and the positive fixtures enforce the restored contract (`TestLastKnownGoodPersistsCleanClientTokenConfig` (r9) and `TestLastKnownGoodPersistsCleanToolMetadataConfig` (r15/C8 — a reference-only `tool_metadata` config ⇒ copy written byte-identical, one `config_loaded`, no warn record, service continues)): clean configs of EVERY class persist.** The entire persist/clear/emission hook body invoking this gate is T3's (r9/C5); T4 consumes the landed hook.
