- **AD23 (NEW at r11 for CT10-C6; precedence amended at r13/K1) — startup admission classifies `admission`/`admission_failed` with a static package-owned cause through a start-exit classifier entry consuming the EXISTING `("rejected","admission_failed")` reload report; NEVER an `internal/service` sentinel.** Rationale: R8 prohibits `internal/service` changes, and `Loop.Start`'s admission-failure path ALREADY reports `("rejected","admission_failed")` through `LoopOptions.ReloadMetrics` (loop.go:157-160 → the cmd-owned hook at serve.go:306) — the one cmd-owned synchronous signal that fires exactly when (and only when) the runtime rejected the STARTUP candidate, strictly before the drivers start (loop.go:180/:195), so no reload can pollute it before a Start exit (verified, §3). Consequences: (1) **T1 defines the classifier hook** in `cmd/mcp-bridge/startup_failure.go` (T1's set): the stage VALUE `admission` joins the closed startup vocabulary (§4.1 — disjoint from `legacyStageVocabulary`, verified); `admissionStartupFailure()` — event `startup_failed` (EXISTING family; NO new event name), operation `startup`, level `error`, outcome `failure`, code `admission_failed`, kind `configuration`, retryable true, static package-owned cause and remediation; and the entry `startupFailureForStartExit(err error, admissionReported bool) startupFailure` with precedence publication-sentinel → catalog-sentinel → admission-capture → **typed-listener (`errors.As`, r13/K1)** → `unclassified`/`internal_error`; `startupFailureForError(err)` KEEPS its signature and delegates with `false` (its call sites serve.go:409 and serve_edge_test.go:193 compile unchanged). (2) **T3 wires the captured outcome into the start-exit classification** in `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve.go` (T3's set): the `ReloadMetrics` hook body (already T3's, r9/C5) additionally sets a SYNCHRONIZED cmd-owned capture boolean on `("rejected","admission_failed")` (mutex/atomic — the hook fires on the loop and watch goroutines); a `fromStartExit` boolean is set ONLY where the classified failure is the `loopErr` Start exit — the select's `failure = <-loopErr` case (drained) and the drain goroutine's `case err := <-loopErr` branch when `firstError` makes that error the failure — and the drain tail calls `startupFailureForStartExit(failure, fromStartExit && captured)`. (3) **Exclusion argument (falsifiable)**: `Loop.Start`'s non-nil returns all precede the driver start (§3), so `fromStartExit ⇒` no reload ever ran `⇒` the capture reflects exactly the startup report at loop.go:159; a publication failure after a successful admission never sets the capture (its sentinel classifies first anyway); reload-sourced captures (loop.go:110) can never pass the gate on a listener/sentinel/ctx path; the load-diagnostics wrap returns upstream via `errors.As`. (4) **Fixtures (ownership settled at r12/J1)**: T1 `TestStartupAdmissionFailureClass` (`cmd/mcp-bridge/startup_failure_test.go`, T1's set — **compile-failure RED under the G3 carve-out since r12/J2: it references `startupFailureForStartExit`/`admissionStartupFailure`, absent pre-implementation**) — the entry with the capture set/unset and the sentinels unchanged; T3 `TestStartupAdmissionFailureClassifiesAdmission` (`cmd/mcp-bridge/serve_edge_test.go`, T3's set — behavioral RED; **AC10's end-to-end admission oracle since r12/J1; AC2's command no longer names it — AC2 is verified by the T1 unit oracle**) — the serve drain tail with a startup admission failure emits stage `admission`/code `admission_failed`/static cause, while other Start failures keep their existing classes. (5) **AC reconciliation**: R1/AC1 restored to genuinely universal (admission included — no carve-out, no owner exception, no acceptance change); AC5 rewritten (see §5) with the second named oracle `TestStartupCleanStartEmitsNoFailure` (the CR10 LOW item); **at r12/J1 AC2's end-to-end clause is re-owned by AC10 — no AC id changes, no weakening**. (6) Rejected alternative: a typed `internal/service` admission sentinel — prohibited by R8, remains an owner-gated non-goal; cmd-side error-text matching — rejected (AD1: raw error text never classifies). (7) The reload-side admission rejection keeps today's behavior (watch `operatorLogf`, no startup record) — the r3–r10 AD17 scope is superseded only at the startup exit.
- **AD24 (NEW at r11 for CT10-H2/C7 and the CR10 advisory items) — every concurrent operator-facing log write serializes through the shared pair; the two raw-writer residuals are DISCLOSED with evidence; the RED lists are split honestly.** (a) **`operatorLogf` routing**: serve.go:198's `operatorLogf := operatorLog(stderr)` becomes `operatorLog(lockedErr)` — the SAME serialized stderr sink the pair/`observe.Logger` use (the pair is constructed once over the process sinks in serve; `operatorLog`'s private wiring.go mutex then nests inside the pair exactly as `Logger.mu` does — lock order stays one-directional `operatorLog.mu` → pair mutex and `Logger.mu` → pair mutex, never reversed). Every `operatorLogf` line — refresher publication-failure warns (refresh.go:455-459), watch reload rejections (loop.go:206-212), and the prompt-publication-rejection closure (serve.go:198-199) — serializes with the Logger, the emission lane, and every other operatorLogf writer. Fixture: `TestOperatorLogfSerializedDuringRefreshPass` (T3's RED set, behavioral RED — the concurrent emission does not exist today; `-race`). (b) **Disclosed residual 1 — the reload-applied `os.Stdout` line**: `operatorLog`'s `configuration reload applied` case overrides `destination = os.Stdout` (wiring.go:485) — a raw `os.Stdout` write outside the pair AND outside the Logger's stdout lock; `wiring.go` is in NO task write set and no owner widening exists for it. Consequence: that one info line may interleave at line level with a concurrent Logger stdout write; it is bounded to stdout (disjoint from every stderr diagnostic), each line a single complete `WriteJSONLine` write (no partial lines), and it is disclosed here and in §7 — not silently claimed safe. (c) **Disclosed residual 2 — the drain-tail final raw diagnostic, with ordering evidence**: the exit/validation writers stay raw (`startup_failure.go:130` — T1's file, but its `main.go:58/:60` callers pass the bare process stderr and `main.go` is unwritable (**r12/J4**: the complete raw `writeStartupFailure` caller set is `main.go:41/45/58/60`; `:83` is inside `checkConfig` — a raw `WriteJSONLine` to stdout, NOT a startup-failure writer); `diagnostics_envelope.go:12`; `wiring.go:519`). Verified: on the DRAINED path the Start exit precedes the driver start, so NO refresher/watch writer ever exists — excluded by construction; on the drain-budget-EXPIRY path the loop may still be running with an active refresher, serve's return fires `cancel()`, the loop/refresher/watch stop at their next `ctx` check, but serve/main never join the loop goroutine — no happens-before edge, so an in-flight pair-held write may still be completing when main's raw `writeStartupFailure` lands: at most LINE-LEVEL ordering nondeterminism between one complete pair-held JSON line and the one complete raw JSON diagnostic on the shared stderr fd — each a single `WriteJSONLine` write (atomic per write; no partial/corrupt lines), and the AD23 capture is synchronized (no data race). Disclosed in §7 exception (5)/§9 for the reviewer to challenge — NOT claimed prevented; no test is weakened to hide it. (d) **C7 — honest RED lists (widened at r12/J2)**: §6 T1/T3 name two buckets — genuine behavioral RED fixtures (must fail as assertion failures, or compile failures ONLY under the G3 carve-out — **three tests at T1 since r12/J2**, pre-implementation) and GREEN-on-arrival compatibility fixtures (baseline preservation: they pass on the PRE-implementation code because the asserted baseline contract already holds — serve.go:111-114/:139-142 + main.go:52-66; nothing reads planted copies or writes any copy today — and they land WITH the change as permanent regression coverage, every final assertion preserved, never weakened, never deleted, never counted as RED). The validation commands STILL RUN THE FULL SET (the patterns already match both buckets). The AC observables and oracles are unchanged by this split — only the RED-step CLAIM is corrected to match what the fixtures can actually do pre-implementation.

## 4. DESIGN
<!-- specdd:design:start -->

**Approach.** Four bounded changes on existing seams: (1) extend the cmd-owned startup-failure envelope with stage/cause and classify the currently-raw exit paths (both `catalog_loop` sources included, **and the startup admission path via AD23's start-exit entry consuming the existing `("rejected","admission_failed")` report**, **and the listener runtime path via the typed `serveListener` error classified by the entry's `errors.As` typed-listener branch (r13/K1)**); (2) add a sibling-file last-known-good copy persisted from the exact validated bytes **gated by the AD22 pre-persist credential scan (r9: server + embedding + client-token values; r15/C8: the COMPLETE resolved-secret inventory — server, embedding, client-token AND `tool_metadata` nested `{env:}`/`{file:}` values — enumerated by the exported `Config.ResolvedSecrets() ([]string, error)` complete-inventory accessor in `internal/config/config.go`, owner widening 3 + the owner secret-inventory decision; the r8 client-token REFUSAL layer is REMOVED; skip + sanitized warn record on any match; clean configs — client-token and tool-metadata-reference alike — persist)**, with a startup-only fallback decision inside the existing `load` closure **whose first successful startup load is memoized as the ONE bootstrap snapshot (AD21), with a test-only load-gate seam (F2, boolean `memo != nil && publishedSettings() == nil` — r10/H4)**; (3) add `MCP_STRICT_CONFIG` ahead of the fallback; (4) add `config_loaded`/`config_degraded`/`config_last_good_write_failed` records emitted from the **cmd-owned reload-report lane** (`LoopOptions.ReloadMetrics`, serve.go:306) — after admission and after successful publication — consuming a plain pending load-outcome slot staged by `load` (AD12 r5; **the entire hook body — persist/clear AND the emission calls, plus AD23's admission capture — is T3's, and T4 consumes the landed hook — r9/C5, superseding the r8/F4 split; every emission/persist write is SERIALIZED through the shared cmd-owned locking writer pair that also backs the `observe.Logger` — r10/H2 — and, at r11/H2, the `operatorLogf` lane routes over the same pair (AD24(a))**). No new dependency, no new metric, no `internal/service` change; **the one `internal/config` change is the owner-widening-3 exported resolver (`ResolvedClientTokens`) plus the r15/C8 owner-approved complete-inventory accessor (`ResolvedSecrets`) in T3's set (envelope scope stated at the TWO EXACT FILES, landed in the canonical state at revision r11 — §10)**; **no `internal/observe/log.go` change (it is in NO task write set)**; no `Dockerfile`.

**4.1 Typed, sanitized startup cause (R1; T1).**

- `startupFailure` (cmd/mcp-bridge/startup_failure.go) gains `stage string` and `cause string`. `writeStartupFailure` sets the new top-level envelope fields (AD8) and passes every `cause` through `sanitizeCause` (one line, control runes → spaces, runs collapsed, cap 200 runes) as defense-in-depth even though all inputs are package-owned static text.
- Closed startup-stage vocabulary: `snapshot_recovery | catalog_loop | listener | publication | admission | state_directory | jwks_cache | embedding_provider | tool_surface | cli | unclassified` — closed (membership asserted by test, AC1) and disjoint from the HTTP diagnostic lane's legacy stage vocabulary (which contains `unknown` and the `*_admission` compounds but NOT `admission` — verified, §3); the unmatched startup class is `unclassified`, so the two share no value.
- New/changed classification (all codes additive; existing codes untouched):
  - `snapshotRecoveryFailure(directory)` — new: code `snapshot_recovery`, kind `resource`, retryable true, stage `snapshot_recovery`, resource{path}. Replaces the raw `return recoveryErr` in the quarantine-recovery block of `serve`.
  - `listenerRuntimeFailure(listener, address)` — new: code `listener_runtime`, kind `resource`, retryable true, stage `listener`, resource{listener,address}. `serveListener` gains the listener name (`inbound`/`operations`) and the configured address, wrapping its `server.Serve` error in a cmd-owned typed error so the drain path can classify it instead of collapsing to `internal_error`. **(r13/K1) REACHABILITY: the typed wrapper is the classifier's input — `startupFailureForStartExit` matches it with `errors.As` and classifies stage `listener`/code `listener_runtime` BEFORE the unclassified fallback (precedence below), so every listener failure that ends `serve` (all of which flow through the drain tail's serve.go:409 call; `serveListener` at :414-420 returns the raw error today, §3) names its stage — the §4.1 constructor and the classifier branch together close the K1 gap.** **(r14/L1) PRODUCER FALSIFIABILITY: `TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass` drives the real producer and the classifier entry as ONE falsifiable chain — a fake `net.Listener` whose `Accept` returns a deterministic non-temporary error (not `http.ErrServerClosed`, so the nil-return branch is not taken) is passed to `serveListener`; the test asserts the returned error IS the cmd-owned wrapper carrying the listener name (`inbound`/`operations`) and the configured address (the underlying `Accept` error remains reachable in the chain for `errors.Is`-style inspection), then asserts `startupFailureForStartExit(err, false)` maps it to stage `listener`/code `listener_runtime`; REMOVING the wrapper — `serveListener` returning its raw `Serve` error, as today — FAILS the test (the wrapper assertion fails and the classifier falls to `unclassified`/`internal_error`), so AC2's producer claim cannot pass with the wrapper absent and the K1 failure mode cannot survive at the producer. The fake listener is a test-local type inside `startup_failure_test.go` (T1's set) — no production seam, no exported API, no new path.**
  - `catalogLoopFailure()` — existing constructor gains stage `catalog_loop` and a static cause; it is constructed for the TWO cmd-owned catalog-loop conditions (AD17): the `service.NewLoop` construction-failure return (serve.go:282-315, constructor called at :314), and the drain-budget sentinel `errCatalogLoopDidNotDrain`, which replaces the inline literal at serve.go:397 and is matched by `errors.Is`. No other `loopErr` value reaches it.
  - **`admissionStartupFailure()` (r11/C6, AD23) — new: code `admission_failed`, kind `configuration`, retryable true, stage `admission`, static package-owned cause + remediation; event `startup_failed` (the EXISTING family — no new event name).**
  - `startupFailureForError` becomes stage-aware and sentinel-aware **and delegates to the START-EXIT ENTRY (r11/C6)**: `startupFailureForError(err)` = `startupFailureForStartExit(err, false)`; **`startupFailureForStartExit(err, admissionReported)`** — `errors.Is(err, service.ErrPublicationFailed)` → `publication` **with stage `publication` and a static cause (E6: this family carries stage AND cause, verified by AC1)**; `errors.Is(err, errCatalogLoopDidNotDrain)` → `catalog_loop`; **`admissionReported` → `admissionStartupFailure()` (AD23 — the captured startup admission outcome)**; **`errors.As(err, &typedListenerErr)` → `listenerRuntimeFailure(typedListenerErr.listener, typedListenerErr.address)` — stage `listener`, code `listener_runtime` (r13/K1: the typed-listener branch sits BEFORE the unclassified fallback — after the admission capture — so no listener failure classifies `internal_error`; a typed-listener error never carries an admission capture because `fromStartExit` is false on every listener path (I14), so the position changes no outcome)**; everything else — genuinely unknown errors, and any start-exit error with NO captured admission report and NO typed listener wrapper — → `fallbackStartupFailure` with stage `unclassified` and the unchanged `internal_error` code (the distinct unclassified class, R1/AC5). `startupFailureForError` KEEPS its signature (its call sites serve.go:409 — swapped by T3 — and serve_edge_test.go:193 compile unchanged).
  - Existing constructors gain their stage (`state_directory`, `jwks_cache`, `embedding_provider`, `tool_surface`, `cli`; `listener_bind` keeps its code and shares stage `listener`).
- **Startup admission errors ARE given a named stage (r11/C6 — this paragraph SUPERSEDES the r3–r10 carve-out; AD23).** `Loop.Start`'s non-nil returns are exactly: load diagnostics (returned as `*config.Diagnostics`, caught by the drain tail's `errors.As` before classification), admission errors (plain), and the `ErrPublicationFailed` sentinel; after publication it returns nil (verified, §3). **With `internal/service` changes prohibited (R8) there is no cmd-visible admission sentinel — but there IS a cmd-visible admission OUTCOME: the existing `("rejected","admission_failed")` reload report fired at loop.go:159 immediately before the startup admission error return, strictly before the drivers start (loop.go:180/:195).** T1 defines the classifier entry `startupFailureForStartExit(err, admissionReported)` + the `admission` stage vocabulary member + `admissionStartupFailure()` (startup_failure.go — T1's); **T3 wires the captured outcome into the start-exit classification** (serve.go — T3's, the file both tasks own): the `ReloadMetrics` hook body sets a synchronized capture boolean on `("rejected","admission_failed")`, a `fromStartExit` gate limits consultation to the `loopErr` Start exit (the select's loopErr case; the drain goroutine's loopErr branch when `firstError` adopts it), and the drain tail classifies `startupFailureForStartExit(failure, fromStartExit && captured)`. **A `Loop.Start` error after a reported startup admission failure therefore classifies `admission`/`admission_failed` with a static cause; every other Start failure keeps its existing class** (load diagnostics → `config_validation_failed` unchanged; publication → `publication`; the drain sentinel → `catalog_loop`; **a typed listener failure → `listener`/`listener_runtime` (r13/K1)**; unknown-without-capture → `unclassified`); **reload-time admission rejections (loop.go:110) keep today's behavior — no startup record, watch logs via `operatorLogf` — and their capture can never pass the `fromStartExit` gate.** No `catalog_loop` label leaks onto any admission error (AD17). A typed admission sentinel in `internal/service` remains an owner-gated follow-up in the non-goals — NOT needed and NOT used by this design. **(r12/J1) Oracle ownership: the admission class is asserted at TWO levels, each inside its owner's write set — the classifier-ENTRY unit by `TestStartupAdmissionFailureClass` (`startup_failure_test.go`, T1's — AC2/AC5), and the END-TO-END wired drain tail by `TestStartupAdmissionFailureClassifiesAdmission` (`serve_edge_test.go`, T3's — AC10). (r13/K1) the listener class is asserted at the classifier-ENTRY unit by `TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass` (`startup_failure_test.go`, T1's — AC2), riding the same drain-tail seam end-to-end as AC10's admission fixture (serve_edge_test.go, T3's). (r14/L1) `TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass` asserts the PRODUCER as well — the fake-listener `Accept` failure through `serveListener` yields the cmd-owned wrapper (name + configured address) before the entry maps it, so the listener oracle chain is producer → wrapper → entry, one named test, and removing the wrapper fails it.**
- **`cause` joins the closed field vocabulary**: `envelopeFieldNames` (envelope.go:587), `docs/logging-contract.md` union, the `log_test.go:46-79` exact-union literal, and the `config_envelope_test.go:166-170` count pin (130 → 131) — all inside T1's owner-widened write set; no other internal/observe test enumerates that union (verified, §3). **(r11/C6) the `admission` stage VALUE and `admission_failed` code change NO field-union member and NO count pin — only the `docs/logging-contract.md` startup classification rows and the vocabulary membership test gain them (T1's set). (r13/K1) likewise the `listener_runtime` code and the typed-listener branch change NO field-union member and NO count pin — `listener` is already a stage value (the `listener_bind` code shares it).**

**4.2 Last-known-good copy (R3; T3).**

- Path: `filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(path), filepath.Base(path)+".last-good.yaml")` — sibling of the operator file (AD2; e.g. `/app/configs/config-network.yaml.last-good.yaml`).
- Content: the exact byte slice the validating load parsed — `load` reads the bytes once (`os.ReadFile(path)` → `config.Parse(data, path)`) and persistence writes that same slice; there is NO re-read of the config file at persistence time, so no unvalidated or concurrently-edited byte can reach the copy (I9) — **and the write happens only if the AD22 credential scan passes those exact bytes (r7/C2 + r9/C4 + r15/C8): the scan checks ALL resolved secret values — server, embedding, client-token, AND `tool_metadata` nested `{env:}`/`{file:}` reference values — enumerated by the exported complete-inventory `Config.ResolvedSecrets()` accessor, before any persist; any resolved-secret match skips the write and emits the sanitized warn record instead. A clean, validated configuration — client-token or `tool_metadata`-reference — persists its copy exactly like any other validated config.** Written atomically (temp file in the same directory → `rename`), mode 0600. Never a serialized/resolved `Config` — `Config.resolved`, `Config.embeddingSnapshot`, and the unexported `clientTokenHashes` hold secret/digest material and must never be written (R7, AD2).
- At load: the copy is always re-read through the standard validation chain (`os.ReadFile(copyPath)` → `config.Parse(copyData, copyPath)`) — never trusted (AD3). Because the copy is a sibling, `{file:}` refs and a relative `storage.directory` resolve against the same directory as the active file, so the served state location is identical to a healthy start of the same document. **No load-time secret scan is required: every copy in existence was written by a post-T3 binary through the AD22-gated persist (r7); pre-T3 binaries write no copies.**
- No persist on a degraded start (AD9); no persist on a load rejected before admission (AD6); persist once per accepted generation sourced from a validated active document (startup and admitted reloads), driven from the emission lane after the `success` report (AD12 r5) **and gated by AD22 (scan match ⇒ skip + warn, never fatal)**; persist failure OR gate skip → one warn `config_last_good_write_failed`, never fatal (AD6/AD22, I8).

**4.3 Startup-load precedence and post-admission emission (R3/R4/R5; T3/T4).**

**Bootstrap snapshot (r7, closes C1; AD21/I13; deterministic seam r8/F2; gate boolean corrected r10/H4).** On every `load` call: **if a bootstrap snapshot is memoized AND the holder's published settings are nil (`memo != nil && holder.publishedSettings() == nil`), the closure returns the memoized snapshot immediately — no disk I/O, no re-parse** (it re-stages the identical `holder.stage` values and the identical pending load-outcome, both idempotent). Otherwise the closure performs steps 1–4 below and, **on success with the holder's published settings still nil, stores the result as the bootstrap snapshot** (candidate, settings, exact validated active bytes, source attribution, resolved credential values). The bootstrap window contains exactly two calls — serve's bootstrap call (serve.go:139) and `Loop.Start`'s call (loop.go:137): `watch`/refresher drivers start strictly after the startup publication (loop.go:172→180/:195), so no reload can occur pre-publication, and every reload (published non-nil) bypasses the memo and loads from disk under the restart-only guard exactly as today (I4). `--check-config` never enters this closure (AD11). **The test-only load gate (F2): the closure invokes the cmd-package-level test hook at its entry, passing the boolean `memo != nil && holder.publishedSettings() == nil` (true exactly on the loop's second pre-publication load; false on every post-publication load — so the gate never arms for the reload path and the AC6(c)/AC14 SIGHUP cases run to their guard rejections, r10/H4); the hook is nil in production (no-op). A fixture's gate blocks the memoized (second) call on a channel until the fixture releases it after mutating the active file — making the inter-load window deterministically holdable and the memo falsifiable (a removed memo makes the second load read the disk and fail the oracles).** Consequence: admission, publication, the outcome slot, the emission, the persistence and the bootstrap-configured resources (storage :154, listeners :317-324, diagnostics/metrics :145-152) all use ONE generation — the bootstrap snapshot (AC14/AC6(c) fixtures; I13).

Inside the existing `load` closure, before the restart-only guard, on the ACTIVE path:

1. Read: `data, err := os.ReadFile(path)`; a read error yields the active document's `config_read` diagnostics — constructed in cmd with the exact `ParseFile` field set (Path, `Key "<document>"`, `Code: "config_read"`, `Property: "document"`, `Shape` not_found/permission_denied/io_error, `Reason: "cannot read configuration file"`, `RemainingStages: "not_checked"`; AD16) — and returns them: NO fallback, no copy read (AD13); existing fail-closed contract (`config_validation_failed`, exit 1, no listener bound). Parity with `config.ParseFile` is asserted by test (AC9).
2. Parse/validate: `candidate, err := config.Parse(data, path)` → `Command()`; validation diagnostics — value-located or document-level (`config_too_large`, AD15) — reach the strict decision: `MCP_STRICT_CONFIG` true (AD4) → return the active diagnostics error unchanged (existing fail-closed contract; **strict is evaluated before the step-3 copy read by evaluation order — I5, a design invariant, not an AC observation**); non-strict → fallback decision (3). **The strict-decision insert at this point is T4's implementation deliverable (R4, ticket 04; r9/C5); the closure it inserts into is T3's — serialize writers T3 before T4.**
3. **Fallback decision (startup load only — published generation nil at load entry, wiring.go:24-93; G1, wording corrected at r8/F8):** copy exists + copy parses+validates (`copyCandidate, copySettings := config.Parse(copyData, copyPath) → Command()`) → **the degraded branch sets `candidate, settings = copyCandidate, copySettings` — the effective candidate and settings for this load — and falls through to step 4. The `load` closure returns the effective CANDIDATE through its unchanged signature (`Load func() (*config.Config, error)` — `Loop.Start`/`Loop.Reload` receive it via `options.Load`, loop.go:137/:103), so `AdmitStartup(candidate)` (loop.go:137-159) admits the degraded copy — the closure never returns the nil active-parse result on the degraded branch (the r5 wording staged the copy directly and left the closure's return undefined; a literal reading returned the nil parse result and exited — the G1 repair). The effective SETTINGS reach the caller through the STAGED HOLDER, never via the Load signature: step 4's `holder.stage(candidate, settings)` (serve.go:136) makes `holder.settings()` (:143) return the copy's settings and the subsequent publication publish them — the caller configures its resources from the holder exactly as serve's bootstrap already does (§3).** The load outcome is marked degraded carrying the ACTIVE rejection attribution (first active diagnostic: path, and its location fields — line/property for value-located diagnostics, or the document-level key/property with no line for `config_too_large`, AD15 — emitted as `config.property_path` `"<document>"`); no persist (AD9). The same effective-candidate assignment applies to the document-level degraded branch (AC6(b)). No copy, or the copy fails to parse/validate/read → return the ACTIVE config's original diagnostics error, never the copy's (AD3): existing fail-closed contract.
4. Valid active OR admitted degraded copy → restart-only guard (reloads only, serve.go:120-135) → `holder.stage(candidate, settings)` (:136) — **staging the EFFECTIVE candidate: on a degraded start this is the copy's candidate (G1), so the publication and every subsequent refresh pass build from the copy exactly as from a healthy start, and the holder hands the effective settings to the caller (F8)**; **the load outcome (effective source: active-with-`data` or copy/degraded; the exact validated active bytes; resolved settings; degraded attribution or none; and — r7, complete since r15/C8 — the full resolved-secret inventory (`ResolvedSecrets()`: server, embedding, client-token AND `tool_metadata` values — the AD22 gate inputs) is staged into the cmd-owned pending load-outcome slot (AD12 r5; **no fingerprint payload — F9: no event or gate consumes one**) — a single mutex-guarded slot (loads are serialized, §3), staged by EVERY successful load, admitted or not; the slot has NO emission semantics and is consumed ONLY by the `success` reload report (T3's hook body — persist/clear and emission calls alike, r9/C5). The most recent load overwrites the slot; a load that fails before a valid candidate stages nothing.**

**Publication-emission invariant (r5, closes E1).** An emission (`config_loaded`/`config_degraded`) or a last-known-good persistence is reachable ONLY through a `success` reload report (`LoopOptions.ReloadMetrics`, serve.go:306) of the load that staged the pending outcome — i.e. only after that load's own admission succeeded and its own publication was accepted (loop.go:122/:168 fire strictly after `options.Published` returned nil). **No publication callback can emit or persist**: the `Published` closure (serve.go:307-309) is byte-identical to today and never touches the outcome slot, so refresh-originated publications (loop.go:81, refresh.go:455) bypass the emission lane entirely. A rejected load (`rejected` report: `invalid_configuration` or `admission_failed`) clears the slot (T3's hook body). A failed publication returns before the success report, so its slot is never consumed (and is overwritten by the next load before any later success). **Therefore a rejected admission can never be followed by an emission or a persistence — for identical content (the E1 case: unchanged-content reload rejected, then a refresh pass publishing the same fingerprint) or changed content alike.** Tested by AC14, including `TestRejectedReloadThenRefreshPublicationEmitsNothing` under `-race`.

**Emission and persistence (AD12 r5; ownership reconciled at r9/C5 — superseding the r8/F4 split; write serialization added at r10/H2; operatorLogf routing added at r11/H2 (AD24(a)); admission capture added at r11/C6 (AD23)).** The cmd-owned `ReloadMetrics` hook (serve.go:306) keeps its existing `metrics.RecordReload(outcome, orNone(reason))` call and additionally, as the emission lane:
(a) **T3's body — the ENTIRE hook body** — on outcome `"success"`: consume the pending load-outcome slot and emit exactly one `config_loaded` (effective document path; resolved absolute storage directory from the outcome's settings, AD7); on a degraded source, then emit `config_degraded` after `config_loaded` (AD5); and invoke the AD22-gated persist of the active document's validated bytes (T3's `last_known_good.go` helper) when — and only when — the outcome's source is a validated active document (AD6/AD9);
(b) on outcome `"rejected"`: clear the slot; no emission, no persistence on the rejected branch (both `invalid_configuration` and `admission_failed`; a restart-only-guard rejection is an `invalid_configuration` load failure) — **also T3's; and — r11/C6/AD23 — on `("rejected","admission_failed")` additionally set the synchronized admission-capture boolean the drain tail consults under the `fromStartExit` gate**;
(c) the hook does not fire for refresh passes, failed publications (which return before the success report), or `publishObserver(nil)` rejected refreshes — verified call-site set, §3.
**Final assertion ownership (r9/C5; admission wiring's oracle ownership settled at r12/J1): T3 implements AND self-verifies the entire hook (persist/clear + emission calls + AD23 capture) with its own fixtures — including `TestDegradedStartOrderLoadedThenDegraded` (AC6(c)/AD5 ordering, **+ the AC14 degraded-branch `resource.path` storage-directory assertion since r12/J5**), `TestLastKnownGoodEmissionSerializedDuringRefreshPass` (r10/H2 write serialization), `TestOperatorLogfSerializedDuringRefreshPass` (r11/H2 operatorLogf serialization) and `TestStartupAdmissionFailureClassifiesAdmission` (r11/C6 start-exit admission wiring — **AC10's end-to-end oracle since r12/J1**) — so every T3 assertion is GREEN with T4 unstarted (strict unset in every T3 fixture; no T3 fixture asserts strict behavior; no T3 command runs a T4 test). T4 consumes the landed hook: its AC14 `TestConfigLoaded*` family, the E1 case and the inter-load consumption fixtures are permanent regression coverage (GREEN on arrival after T3 — not RED, not weakened), and its RED set is the strict family, the envelope matrix and the doc/G2 tests. `blockedBy T3` unchanged.**
The `Published` closure itself is UNCHANGED (`publishAcceptedAssembly(assembly, promptSurface.Published, surface.Published, published)`, serve.go:307-309): prompts → projection → observer, no emission, no persistence, no slot access (D5's ordering — projections first, emission only after the chain accepts — now holds by construction, since the emission lane fires only after the chain returned nil).

Reloads (published generation non-nil at load entry) are unchanged: no fallback, invalid candidates rejected, published generation preserved (I4). Degraded-start staging consequence (G1): the admitted candidate and published settings are the copy's; when the operator fixes the active file, a SIGHUP reload admits it unless a restart-only field differs from the copy's settings (`restart_required`, existing rule) — correct and safe. **Bootstrap consequence (r7/AD21): an inter-load A→B edit changes nothing until a reload can legally see it — post-publication, under the restart-only guard; the bootstrap generation is the one served, reported and persisted (I13); the F2 gate makes that assertion deterministic in tests.**

**4.4 Events (R3/R5; T3 owns ALL THREE writers and the emission calls — r9/C5; T4 consumes the emission and owns only the strict-mode/startup-envelope assertions and the contract docs).** All three on the existing Envelope+`observe.WriteJSONLine` lane used by `writeConfigValidationFailure`/`writeStartupFailure` (writers in cmd/mcp-bridge/diagnostics_envelope.go — **all three T3's since r9: `writeConfigLoaded`, the `config_degraded` writer, the `config_last_good_write_failed` writer** — invoked from the reload-report emission lane per AD12, after admission and after successful publication; no `eventMessage`/`Fields`-allowlist change — AD8). **(r10/H2) SERIALIZED SINKS: serve constructs one cmd-owned locking writer pair (a `serializedSink`-style `io.Writer` wrapper declared in `diagnostics_envelope.go`, T3's set — mutex + inner writer, `Write` under the lock) over the process sinks, constructs the refresher/HTTP `observe.Logger` over the SAME pair (serve.go:108), and the three writers marshal via `observe.WriteJSONLine` INTO the pair — never into the bare process sinks. The Logger path (`l.mu` → pair mutex) and the emission path (pair mutex) are mutually serialized at the byte level: no interleaved or partial lines, no data race on buffer-backed sinks under `-race`. Lock order one-directional (`l.mu` → pair mutex) — no deadlock. `internal/observe/log.go` is NOT modified (no task owns it); level routing preserved (info→stdout, warn→stderr — the pair is a stdout/stderr pair; each writer uses its level's sink). Enforced by `TestLastKnownGoodEmissionSerializedDuringRefreshPass` (AC-scope, T3's `-race` command 1). (r11/H2, AD24(a)) THE OPERATOR LOGF LANE JOINS THE SAME SERIALIZATION: serve.go:198's `operatorLogf := operatorLog(stderr)` becomes `operatorLog(lockedErr)` — the same serialized stderr sink — so the refresher publication-failure warns (refresh.go:455-459), the watch reload rejections (loop.go:206-212) and the prompt-publication-rejection closure serialize with the Logger, the emission lane, and each other (`operatorLog`'s private mutex nests inside the pair one-directionally, exactly as `Logger.mu` does). Enforced by `TestOperatorLogfSerializedDuringRefreshPass` (T3's `-race` command 1). TWO DISCLOSED RESIDUALS (AD24(b)/(c)): the `configuration reload applied` line's `destination = os.Stdout` override (wiring.go:485 — a raw stdout write outside the pair and the Logger's stdout lock; `wiring.go` is unwritable; bounded to that one info line on stdout, disjoint from every stderr diagnostic); and the raw exit/validation writers (`startup_failure.go:130`, `main.go:41/45/58/60` — the raw `writeStartupFailure` callers, r12/J4; `main.go:83` inside `checkConfig` is a stdout `WriteJSONLine`, not a startup-failure writer; `diagnostics_envelope.go:12`, `wiring.go:519`) — with the drain-tail ordering evidence: the DRAINED path excludes refresher writers by construction (the Start exit precedes the driver start), the drain-budget-EXPIRY path has no happens-before join (an in-flight pair-held write may complete alongside main's raw final diagnostic — at most line-level ordering nondeterminism between two complete single-write JSON lines on the shared stderr fd; no partial lines, no data race on the synchronized capture).**

- `config_loaded` — level `info`, operation `load_config`, outcome `success`, static message; `config.path` = the effective document path (active on a normal generation, the copy on a degraded start); `resource.path` = resolved absolute storage directory (AD7). Exactly one per accepted generation (AD12 r5: consumed from the pending load-outcome slot by the `success` report; never from a publication callback; never on a refresh pass; never on a publication failure). **On a startup generation the reported path/directory are the bootstrap snapshot's (AD21/I13).**
- `config_degraded` — level `warn`, operation `load_config`, outcome `degraded`, static message; `error.code` = the rejected record's diagnostic code (the class of the rejected value), kind `configuration`; `config` = `ConfigInfo{Path: active path, PropertyPath, Line, Column, Server, Shape, DiagnosticsIncomplete, RemainingStages}` from the first active diagnostic — value-located diagnostics carry line/property; document-level diagnostics (`config_too_large`) carry `Key "<document>"`/property `document` with no line, **emitted as `config.property_path` = the literal `"<document>"` (the verbatim `PropertyPath = record.Key` mapping, AD15 r6/G4)**; `resource.path` = resolved absolute storage directory. Exactly one per degraded start, emitted after `config_loaded` (AD5). Because the fallback fires only on validation diagnostics of a readable document (AD13), the degraded record always carries attribution.
- `config_last_good_write_failed` — level `warn`, operation `load_config`, outcome `failure`; `error.code` = `last_good_write_failed`, kind `resource`; `resource.path` = the copy path. One per failed persist attempt **and one per AD22 scan skip (r7/C2 + r9/C4): the family is reused for both skip/failure classes — persist I/O failure and credential-scan skip (any `ResolvedSecrets()` match — server, embedding, client-token, or `tool_metadata` value; the r8 refusal class is removed with the refusal layer) — same level/operation/outcome/code/kind/path, static package-owned message naming which class; the record never carries the matched bytes, the credential reference, or any secret (sanitized like every cmd record — AD1/AD22).**

**4.5 Ticket-02 consumption (R2; T2).** With the T1 build, the instance's generic `internal_error` line is replaced by a `stage`/`error.code`/`cause` triple. T2 works entirely from the owner-lane-supplied instance evidence — the recorded container state, the last stderr lines of a crash cycle, and a local copy of the instance's ACTUAL config and state layout (AD18; no network, SSH, docker or registry access is granted) — reproduces the failure locally against that actual layout (not a reduced reproduction), captures the typed `stage`/`error.code`/`cause` line BEFORE the fix by running the **post-T3 binary** (admission capture wired by T3, AD23 — landed before T2 per the r17/C9 writer order T1 → T3 → T2 → T4) against that actual layout (**AC17 — the owner-APPROVED lane, AD20; no live effect granted**), records stage/code/cause plus the discriminating config/state facts, the quoted local typed line, and the live instance's literal generic line + `RestartCount` evidence verbatim in the ticket, lands the CODE fix inside T2's bound write set (§10) with the regression test `TestNetworkStartupRecoveryClass` (the packet's delivered remedy, E4), and reports. Live verification (AC18) is owner/operator-lane after delivery. If the diagnosis concludes the fault is configuration-only, T2 records the exact follow-up (AD14) and reports its remedy step blocked-on-owner.

**Invariants.** (I1) `stage`/`cause` never contain runtime error text (AD1). (I2) the copy is always re-validated at load, never trusted (AD3). (I3) the bridge never writes the operator's config file — only the sibling copy is created/replaced. (I4) reloads never fall back; a load rejected before admission emits and persists nothing — and per the publication-emission invariant (r5) can never be followed by an emission from any later publication. (I5) **strict disables the fallback before any copy read — a design invariant enforced by evaluation order (§4.3 step 2 precedes step 3), not an AC observation (r8/F7: AC12 no longer claims it).** (I6) every envelope addition is additive; no existing code/event/level/outcome is repurposed. (I7) no secret value reaches the copy or any event — **enforced at the persist gate by the AD22 credential scan (server + embedding + client-token values since r9; the COMPLETE `ResolvedSecrets()` inventory — adding the `tool_metadata` values — since r15/C8), not inferred from credential-field validation**. (I8) persistence failure OR AD22 scan skip never fails startup or the reload (AD6/AD22). (I9) the copy is written from the exact validated bytes; there is no persistence-time re-read. (I10) `catalog_loop` labels only the two cmd-owned catalog-loop conditions (AD17). (I11) **the `Published` closure is emission-free; emission/persistence occur only on the `success` reload report of the load that staged the outcome (AD12 r5).** (I12, r6/G1; wording corrected at r8/F8) **the `load` closure hands its caller exactly the settings it validated and intends to serve on every serving branch — the effective CANDIDATE travels via the closure's unchanged `Load` signature, the effective SETTINGS travel through the staged holder (`holder.stage`/`holder.settings()`): the degraded branch's returned candidate and holder-served settings are the copy's (`candidate, settings = copyCandidate, copySettings`), and the candidate staged by step 4 and admitted by `AdmitStartup` is that effective candidate; the fail-closed branches return the active diagnostics unchanged.** (I13, r7/C1/AD21; deterministic at r8/F2; gate boolean corrected at r10/H4) **one bootstrap generation end-to-end: while the holder's published settings are nil, every `load` call returns the memoized bootstrap snapshot without disk I/O — so the loop's startup admission, publication, outcome slot, emission, persistence and the bootstrap-configured resources (storage, listeners, diagnostics, metrics) all use the SAME generation; an inter-load A→B edit is admitted, persisted and reported by no path, and the test-only load gate (nil in production, armed on `memo != nil && publishedSettings() == nil`) makes that assertion deterministic.** (I14, r11/C6/AD23) **the admission capture is consulted ONLY for the `loopErr` Start exit (`fromStartExit` gate): no listener, sentinel, or ctx-path classification ever sees it, and a reload-sourced `admission_failed` report can never reach a start-exit classification because `Loop.Start`'s non-nil exits all precede the first reload. (r13/K1 corollary) the typed-listener branch is therefore evaluated with `admissionReported` false on every listener path — the branch order after the admission capture is outcome-neutral.**

**Failure behavior.** Active document unreadable → no fallback → fail closed with the active `config_read` diagnostics, field-identical to `ParseFile`'s (AD13/AD16). Copy unreadable/unvalidatable at load → no fallback → fail closed with the active diagnostics (AD3). Persist failure → warn record, startup/reload continues (AD6). **AD22 scan match (any `ResolvedSecrets()` value — server, embedding, client-token, or `tool_metadata` reference value — in the validated bytes) → persistence skipped, warn record, startup/reload continues; the copy keeps the previous validated generation (AD22, r9; complete inventory r15/C8).** Event write failure → the existing `WriteLoggingFailureFallback` lane, unchanged. **Admission failure after a degraded decision → the startup `("rejected","admission_failed")` report clears the slot AND sets AD23's capture; the generation is never published and never emitted; the process exits via the ADMISSION class — stage `admission`, code `admission_failed`, static cause (r11/C6) — the degraded branch's effective candidate is admitted first and rejected by admission like any candidate (G1 does not bypass admission).** **Listener runtime failure → `serveListener`'s typed wrapper flows through the drain tail (serve.go:409) and classifies stage `listener`, code `listener_runtime`, resource{listener,address} — never `unclassified`/`internal_error` (r13/K1).** Publication (projection) failure → returns before the success report; the slot is never consumed; `startup_publication_failed` (stage `publication`, static cause — E6) is emitted via the existing drain classification; NO `config_loaded`/`config_degraded`/persist for that generation (AC14). Refresh pass → `Published` runs the projections, emits/persists nothing (AD12 r5). **Inter-load active-file edit (A→B) during the bootstrap window → ignored: the memoized loop load returns the bootstrap snapshot (A); B reaches the process only through a post-publication reload, where the restart-only guard governs it (AD21/I13; the F3 fixture B changes `storage.directory`, so that SIGHUP is guard-rejected `restart_required`).**

**Alternatives rejected (meaningful tradeoffs only).** (a) Serializing the resolved `Config` as the fallback — rejected: `resolved`/`embeddingSnapshot` hold secret values and the relative-path base would break (R7, AD2). (b) Storing the copy under `storage.directory` — rejected: the storage directory is config-derived and unresolvable before a successful parse — the exact chicken-and-egg the fallback exists to break. (c) Redacted truncated `err.Error()` as `cause` — rejected (AD1). (d) Falling back on `Loop.Reload` — rejected: would mask invalid reload candidates and change reload semantics (MB-006 territory). (e) Emitting the new events on the `Logger.Log`/Entry lane — rejected: it would require widening the closed `eventMessage` switch and the `Fields` allowlist; **(r10/H2) routing the three envelope writers through the `observe.Logger` instance's own mutex is likewise impossible without an `internal/observe` change — `Logger`'s only exported write method is `Log(Entry)` (log.go:85); the shared cmd-owned locking pair (in T3's set) serializes the Logger's writes and the emission lane's writes with zero `internal/observe` change.** (f) **The r4 fingerprint-keyed pending-admission cell consumed inside the `Published` closure — rejected at r5 (E1): the closure cannot discriminate admission from refresh publications when content is unchanged, and the cell was armed before admission; the reload-report lane replaces it (AD12 r5).** (g) A cmd-side `sync.Once` emission inside `load` — rejected: emission before admission/publish accepts nothing; withdrawn since r1. (h) Splitting the Refresher's `Published` from the admission `Published` — rejected: requires an `internal/service` change (loop.go:81), prohibited by R8. (i) **Returning the active (nil) parse result from the degraded branch while staging only the copy — rejected at r6 (G1): `AdmitStartup` would admit a nil candidate and exit; the effective-candidate assignment and return are the repair.** (j) **Fingerprint-compare-and-fail-closed at the loop's startup load (r7, C1) — rejected: it converts a benign mid-startup operator edit into a new startup failure class, adds a rejection semantic to the bootstrap path, and still needs the bytes re-read; the AD21 memoized snapshot achieves one generation with no new failure behavior.** (k) **A `LoopOptions`/loop-side bootstrap-candidate field (r7, C1) — rejected: an `internal/service` change, prohibited by R8; the cmd-owned closure memo achieves the same contract.** (l) **A dedicated `config_last_good_persist_skipped` event or a load-time copy scan (r7, C2) — rejected: reusing the existing `config_last_good_write_failed` family keeps the event vocabulary closed (AD22), and no load-time scan is required because no copy can pre-date the AD22-gated persist.** (m) **A timing/sleep-based inter-load fixture — rejected (r8, CR7 F2): non-deterministic and passes with the memo removed; the explicit F2 gate is the falsifiable seam. An exported `internal/config` client-token resolver — rejected at r8 for lack of owner authorization; NOT rejected at r9: the owner approved widening 3 and the resolver (`Config.ResolvedClientTokens()`) IS the r9 design (C4).** (n) **Refusing persistence for every configured `client_auth.bearer_tokens` (the r8/F1 layer) — rejected at r9 (CT8 C4): configuration-based refusal is not value-based safety and it contradicts ticket `issues/03:14` (validated configs persist); the scan path restores the contract with strictly stronger secret coverage. Kept out only where the owner says so — no alternative refusal layer may be reintroduced without a fresh owner decision.** (o, r11/C6) **A typed `internal/service` admission sentinel — rejected (R8, prohibited; remains an owner-gated non-goal): the cmd-side capture of the EXISTING `("rejected","admission_failed")` report classifies the startup admission exit with zero `internal/service` change (AD23).** (p, r11/C6) **Consulting the admission capture without the `fromStartExit` gate — rejected: a reload-sourced capture (loop.go:110) could misclassify a concurrent listener failure as `admission`; the gate confines consultation to the `loopErr` Start exit, where no reload has ever run (I14).** (q, r13/K1) **Leaving the listener class at `unclassified`/`internal_error` (the r11–r12 precedence) — rejected (CR12 K1): every listener failure that ends `serve` flows through the drain-tail classifier (serve.go:409; `serveListener` at :414-420 returns a raw error today), so without a typed wrapper and an `errors.As` branch a likely-live class stays undiagnosed, violating R1's universal; a `sentinel-errors.Is` match is rejected in favor of the typed wrapper because `server.Serve` returns arbitrary wrapped errors that no cmd-owned sentinel can key on, while the typed wrapper carries the listener name/address the resource fields need.** (r, r14/L1) **Asserting the classifier entry on a hand-constructed typed error only (the r13 fixture shape) — rejected (CR13 L1): the producer `serveListener` would remain unverified, so AC2 could pass with the wrapper absent and the K1 failure mode would survive at the producer; the fake-`net.Listener` producer assertions inside the SAME named test close the gap with zero id/path/command change.**

<!-- specdd:design:end -->

## 5. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA

All ACs are behavioral assertions an executor can verify without reading intent; no AC is weakened to reach GREEN. Test names below are normative; the executor may add cases but not rename or narrow these. Every `-run` pattern uses a single `|` (Go regex alternation); no `\|` literal appears in any command. **(r12/J1) Oracle-ownership rule: every AC's named test lives in its owner task's write set — or, where an AC's oracle lives in a dependency's file (AC14's T3-file fixtures), the owner is `blockedBy` that dependency, so the test is guaranteed present when the AC's verification commands run; no `-run` pattern can pass on an absent test.**

| AC | Req | Ticket | Observable pass/fail | Verification |
|----|-----|--------|----------------------|--------------|
| AC1 | R1 | 01 | A startup error that is not a config diagnostic emits `error.code` naming the stage plus non-empty bounded `stage` and a single-line `cause` on every startup-failure record — **including the `startup_publication_failed` family: stage `publication` plus a non-empty static `cause` (E6)** **and including a startup admission failure: stage `admission`, code `admission_failed`, static package-owned cause (r11/C6/AD23 — no carve-out, no owner exception)** **and including a listener runtime failure: stage `listener`, code `listener_runtime`, resource{listener,address} (r13/K1)**; every emitted `stage` is a member of the closed startup-stage vocabulary, which is disjoint from the HTTP lane's stage values (no `unknown` collision). | `go test ./cmd/mcp-bridge -run 'TestStartupFailureStageCause|TestStartupStageVocabularyMembership|TestPublicationFailureCarriesStageAndCause|TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass' -count=1` |

`snapshot_recovery`/code `snapshot_recovery`; stage `listener`/code `listener_runtime` (r13/K1: the STAGE is `listener`, the CODE `listener_runtime` — the r12 parenthetical named `listener_runtime` as if it were the stage, a spec defect corrected here); stage `catalog_loop`/code `catalog_loop`; stage `admission`/code `admission_failed`**; load diagnostics keep the `*config.Diagnostics` path and the drain-tail clause is re-owned by AC10 (T3, whose set carries `serve_edge_test.go`) — AC2's command names only T1-owned fixtures.** | `go test ./cmd/mcp-bridge -run 'TestSnapshotRecoveryFailureClass|TestCatalogLoopDrainSentinelClass|TestCatalogLoopNewLoopFailureClass|TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass|TestStartupAdmissionFailureClass' -count=1` |

(b) A document-level rejection — an oversized active document (`config_too_large`, `Key "<document>"`, no line) with a valid copy ⇒ the same degraded start (same effective-candidate assignment and holder-mediated settings) with document-level attribution: active path, **`config.property_path` = the literal `"<document>"` (the verbatim `PropertyPath = record.Key` mapping, AD15 r6/G4 — the diagnostic's `Property: "document"` names the class and maps to no envelope field)**, no line, class `config_too_large`. **(c, r7/C1 + r8/F2; gate boolean r10/H4) Inter-load mutation, degraded start — deterministic seam: the active document A fails value-located validation and a valid persisted copy C exists; the fixture arms the test-only load gate (cmd-package-level hook in `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve.go`, nil in production; assigned by the fixture, reset via `t.Cleanup` — hook lifetime is the test's; the gate boolean is `memo != nil && publishedSettings() == nil`), starts serve, and the gate BLOCKS the loop's startup load (the memoized second pre-publication call, held on a channel) until the fixture rewrites the ACTIVE file and releases the gate. Asserted (all under `-race`): the served settings are C's; exactly one `config_degraded` naming A's active rejection; exactly one `config_loaded` naming the COPY path and C's resolved absolute storage directory; no persistence (AD9). Memo falsifier: if the memo is removed, the second load reads the disk, admits the valid mutated file, and the copy-path/`config_degraded` assertions FAIL — the fixture is falsifiable, not vacuous (`TestDegradedStartInterLoadMutationKeepsBootstrapCopy`).** **(r9/C5) All AC6 fixtures are T3-owned and reach GREEN with T4 unstarted — the `config_loaded`/`config_degraded` records they observe are emitted by T3's own hook body.** | `go test -race ./cmd/mcp-bridge -run 'TestDegradedStartServesLastKnownGood|TestDegradedStartDocumentLevelAttribution|TestDegradedStartInterLoadMutationKeepsBootstrapCopy' -count=1` |

(vi) `TestLastKnownGoodPersistsCleanToolMetadataConfig` — POSITIVE: a valid reference-only `tool_metadata` configuration (no resolved value anywhere in its bytes) ⇒ the copy IS written byte-identical to the validated bytes, no warn record, exactly one `config_loaded`, service continues — the ticket-03 validated-config-persists contract holds for the class.** The scan covers every resolved server/embedding credential value (per-server `ServerCredentials` bearer + header values, `EmbeddingCredential`), every resolved client-token value (`ResolvedClientTokens()`, r9), AND every resolved `tool_metadata` nested `{env:}`/`{file:}` reference value — all enumerated by the exported complete-inventory `ResolvedSecrets()` accessor (r15/C8; the `_meta` shape gate tool_metadata.go:140-148 permitting arbitrary nested mappings; the generic reference walk diagnostics.go:271-448, `resolveSecrets` ~:630-648, retained on `Config.resolved` — config.go:68,384,455) — skipping only unresolvable/empty values; a resolver resolution error contributes no scan input (unreachable for validated candidates — `reference_unresolved`). **(r9/C5) All AC8 fixtures are T3-owned (the r8 refusal fixture `TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnClientTokenConfigured` is removed WITH the removed refusal layer — superseded by (iv), not weakened).** | `go test ./cmd/mcp-bridge -run 'TestLastKnownGoodCopyHasNoSecrets|TestConfigDegradedEventHasNoSecrets|TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnSecretInComment|TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnSecretInNonCredentialText|TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnClientTokenInComment|TestLastKnownGoodPersistSkippedOnToolMetadataSecretInComment|TestLastKnownGoodPersistsCleanClientTokenConfig|TestLastKnownGoodPersistsCleanToolMetadataConfig' -count=1` |
| AC9 | R3 | 03 | An active document that cannot be read (`config_read`), or an invalid active with no copy (or a copy that fails parse/validation) ⇒ exit non-zero with `config_validation_failed` carrying the ACTIVE config's diagnostics; no fallback attempted; no listener bound. The cmd read-error diagnostics are field-for-field identical to `config.ParseFile`'s `config_read` records (Path, Key `"<document>"`, Code, Property, Shape, Reason, RemainingStages) for every read-error shape the environment can produce on a shared fixture (at minimum not_found and io_error via a directory path; permission_denied where permissions are enforceable). | `go test ./cmd/mcp-bridge -run 'TestInvalidConfigWithoutFallbackFailsClosed|TestConfigReadErrorNeverFallsBack|TestConfigReadErrorParityWithParseFile' -count=1` |
| AC10 | R3 | 03 | A validating active config is persisted as the new copy from its exact validated bytes once per accepted generation (startup and admitted reloads; atomic, 0600, no operator-file rewrite, no persistence-time re-read, driven from the `success` report after publication via **T3's hook body — the AD22 credential scan (the COMPLETE resolved-secret inventory — server, embedding, client-token AND `tool_metadata` values — via the exported `ResolvedSecrets()` accessor, r15/C8) runs inside that body: a match skips the write and warns (AC8), never fails; a clean client-token config persists (AC8(iii))**); a reload rejected before admission does NOT overwrite the copy (**T3's clear-on-rejected body clears the slot**); a corrupt copy is re-validated and never served; a persist failure warns (`config_last_good_write_failed`) and startup continues. **(r12/J1) END-TO-END ADMISSION WIRING — re-owned from AC2: a `Loop.Start` admission exit carrying the captured `("rejected","admission_failed")` report classifies through the WIRED serve drain tail as stage `admission`/code `admission_failed` with a static package-owned cause (r11/C6/AD23) — asserted by `TestStartupAdmissionFailureClassifiesAdmission` in `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve_edge_test.go` (T3's set — the same file as the hook body and the `fromStartExit` gate it observes; behavioral RED pre-T3-implementation, matched by T3 validation command 1's `TestStartupAdmission` prefix); while other Start failures keep their existing classes (load diagnostics → `config_validation_failed`; publication sentinel → `publication`), and a reload admission rejection never produces a startup record.** **(r9/C5) The ENTIRE hook body — persist/clear AND the `config_loaded`/`config_degraded` emission calls (and, r11, AD23's admission capture) — is T3's; T4 consumes the landed hook and owns no code inside it. - **Allowed scope.** The stage/cause additions, the new constructors/classifications (`snapshot_recovery`, `listener_runtime`, **`admission`/`admission_failed` (AD23)**; `catalog_loop` stage/cause on the `service.NewLoop` failure branch AND the drain-budget sentinel at serve.go:397, AD17), the publication family's stage/cause (E6), **the start-exit classifier entry `startupFailureForStartExit` and the `startupFailureForError` delegation (AD23), INCLUDING the `errors.As` typed-listener branch (r13/K1)**, the cmd-owned typed `serveListener` error wrapper carrying the listener name + configured address (r13/K1), `sanitizeCause`, the vocabulary membership test (**with the `admission` member**), the count-pin update, and their tests/docs — **including the test-local fake `net.Listener` and the `serveListener` producer assertions inside `TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass` (r14/L1 — a test-local type in T1's own test file; no production seam, no exported API)**. **Prohibited scope.** No change to `internal/service`, `internal/upstream`, `internal/storage`, `internal/config`, `internal/search`, `internal/auth`; **no admission capture/wiring in serve (that is T3's — T1 defines only the classifier entry and its unit fixtures; the ReloadMetrics hook body and the :409 call-site swap are T3's)**; no admission classification of any reload/listener/sentinel failure (the entry classifies admission ONLY on the passed `admissionReported` boolean — T1's fixtures pass it explicitly); no `catalog_loop` classification of any `loopErr` value except the drain sentinel; no new event names on the Entry lane (the admission record reuses `startup_failed`); no new metric; no exit-code change.
- **RED step (corrected at r6 for G3; split honestly at r11 for C7/AD24(d); carve-out widened at r12 for J2; branch coverage extended at r13 for K1; producer coverage added at r14 for L1).** **Genuine behavioral RED (must fail as assertion failures pre-implementation):** `TestStartupFailureStageCause`, `TestStartupStageVocabularyMembership` (gains the `admission` member), `TestPublicationFailureCarriesStageAndCause` (E6), `TestSnapshotRecoveryFailureClass`, `TestCatalogLoopNewLoopFailureClass`, `TestStartupUnknownErrorKeepsUnknownClass` (asserts the r11 AC5 semantics: no captured admission report and no typed wrapper ⇒ `unclassified`), `TestStartupCauseSanitized`, `TestStartupFailureNeverEchoesSecrets`. **Compile-failure RED (the G3 carve-out — THREE tests since r12/J2):** `TestCatalogLoopDrainSentinelClass` (`errCatalogLoopDidNotDrain`), `TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass` (the new cmd-owned listener typed error — **and, r14/L1, the new classifier entry symbols its producer+entry assertions reference; the fixture compiles only when the wrapper declaration lands in the same RED step**), **`TestStartupAdmissionFailureClass` (r12/J2 — reason: the fixture exercises the capture-set branch of the new `startupFailureForStartExit(err, admissionReported)` entry and `admissionStartupFailure()` (AD23(1)/(2)); neither symbol is declared pre-implementation, so the fixture cannot compile until the classifier entry lands in the same RED step — its assertions (capture set ⇒ `admission`/`admission_failed`/static cause; unset ⇒ `unclassified`; sentinels unchanged) then hold as the permanent AC2/AC5 unit oracle)** — may legitimately fail RED as compile failures until those declarations land in the same RED step, and no other T1 test. **(r13/K1) `TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass`'s assertions are the AC2 typed-listener oracle: the entry classifies the typed wrapper (listener name + address) as stage `listener`/code `listener_runtime` with the capture unset and the sentinels unchanged; the wrapper and the `errors.As` branch land in the same RED step as the fixture's declarations. (r14/L1) its producer assertions — the fake-listener `Accept` failure through `serveListener` yielding the cmd-owned wrapper — are part of the same fixture: pre-implementation they cannot compile (the wrapper type is absent), post-implementation they FAIL if the wrapper is removed from `serveListener`, which is exactly the falsifiability CR13 demanded.** **GREEN-on-arrival compatibility (lands WITH the change as permanent regression coverage; never counted as RED; never weakened):** **`TestStartupCleanStartEmitsNoFailure` (r11 — a clean `Loop.Start` return already emits no startup-failure record today; it asserts the AC5 clean-start clause and keeps it oracle-verified as production changes).** No test is written to fail for any other reason.

**T2 — Local diagnosis and code fix of the `mcp-bridge-network` loop (ticket 02; single engineer-owned task; live verification is owner-lane, AD18).**
- **Objective.** R2 within the owner-APPROVED local lane (AD20): consume the owner-supplied instance evidence, reproduce the failure locally against the instance's actual config and state layout, record the diagnosis (stage/code/cause + discriminating facts + the quoted local typed line + the live instance's literal generic line and `RestartCount` evidence + **the APPROVED evidence-lane decision reference, AD20**) in the ticket BEFORE the fix, land the CODE fix (the packet's delivered remedy, E4) with the regression test `TestNetworkStartupRecoveryClass` in `cmd/mcp-bridge/network_startup_recovery_test.go`, and record the result. AC17 evidence is local and file-based; AC18 live evidence is produced by the owner/operator lane after delivery and bound by the coordinator (AD18).
- **Dependencies.** **blockedBy T1 and T3 (canonical `blockedBy: ["T1","T3"]` — the T3 edge added at r17/C9, a dependency binding applied in the feature plan record `plan.json` and carried into the machine state with the r17 projection; not a new task)**: the typed cause must exist (T1) AND the admission capture must be wired in the drain tail (T3 — the `startupFailureForStartExit(failure, fromStartExit && captured)` swap at serve.go:409, AD23) before the failure can be identified. **T2's pre-fix capture runs on the POST-T3 binary, whose wired admission diagnostic is proven by T3's end-to-end oracle `TestStartupAdmissionFailureClassifiesAdmission` (`cmd/mcp-bridge/serve_edge_test.go`, AC10) being GREEN before T2 dispatches — T1's classifier unit fixture (`TestStartupAdmissionFailureClass`) is NOT sufficient: the capture is wired by T3 (AD23) and landed before T2, so the drain tail does NOT pass admission capture false at T2's execution point.** Shares `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve.go`/`startup_failure.go` seams with T1/T3/T4 — **writer order below (one writer at a time: T1 → T3 → T2 → T4)**.
- **Bounded steps.** (1) Consume the owner-lane-supplied instance evidence: the recorded container state, the last stderr lines of a crash cycle, and a local copy of the instance's actual config + state layout (`/srv/mcp-grafnea/mcp_bridge_network_data → /app/configs` bind shape, `storage.directory: ./data`); no network, SSH, docker or registry access is granted or attempted. (2) Run the **post-T3 binary** (T1's classifier entry + T3's wired admission capture, AD23 — landed before T2 per the r17/C9 writer order T1 → T3 → T2 → T4; its wired admission diagnostic is proven GREEN by T3's end-to-end oracle `TestStartupAdmissionFailureClassifiesAdmission`, AC10, before T2 dispatches) against that actual config + state layout (local reproduction) and capture the typed `stage`/`error.code`/`cause` line. (3) Record the diagnosis in the ticket file `.scratch/config-admission-resilience/issues/02-diagnose-and-fix-network-startup-loop.md`: stage, code, cause, the discriminating config/state facts, the quoted local typed line, the live instance's literal generic line and `RestartCount` evidence verbatim, **and the APPROVED evidence-lane decision reference (AD20, `.omp/artifacts/config-admission-resilience/owner-ticket02-evidence.json`) — no PENDING marker for this lane** — BEFORE the fix. (4) Land the code fix inside the four bound write paths with the regression test. (5) Record the result; if the diagnosis concludes configuration-only, record the exact follow-up (AD14) and report the remedy step blocked-on-owner (E4).
- **Expected files (write set — mirrors the canonical state exactly; 4 paths).** `.scratch/config-admission-resilience/issues/02-diagnose-and-fix-network-startup-loop.md` (the ticket/diagnosis record), `cmd/mcp-bridge/serve.go`, `cmd/mcp-bridge/startup_failure.go`, `cmd/mcp-bridge/network_startup_recovery_test.go`. No other path is writable by T2: no `internal/**`, no other `cmd/mcp-bridge` test file, no `docs/**`, no `configs/**`, no `CHANGELOG.md`, **no `Dockerfile`** — a needed seam outside the set is reported BLOCKED-with-evidence and requires explicit owner authorization.
- **Allowed scope / effects.** Local build and test execution within the write set; writing the ticket record; the code fix and regression test. **Prohibited effects (granted to NO task in this packet).** Image/registry push (including `registry.bullprog.com:5000`); container redeploy/restart; live SSH or network inspection of any host or container; `docker inspect`/docker daemon access; inbound MCP verification requests; ≥10-minute live `RestartCount` observation — all live steps are owner/operator-lane (AD18); **the AD20 approval grants none of them**. **Prohibited scope.** Deleting or disabling the service; dropping configured pins/upstreams; changing restart supervision policy; writing any deployed/host configuration (owner-gated operator follow-up only, AD14); writing outside the four bound paths.
- **RED step.** `TestNetworkStartupRecoveryClass` fails as an assertion failure on the pre-fix code (code class only; a configuration-only conclusion records the owner-gated follow-up instead and reports the remedy step blocked-on-owner — E4/AC19).
- **AC ids.** AC17, AC18, AC19 (AC20 suite).
- **Required validation commands (all exit 0).** 1. `go test ./cmd/mcp-bridge -run 'TestNetworkStartupRecoveryClass' -count=1` (fails pre-fix, passes post-fix); 2. the AC20 suite (`go test -race -p 1 ./...`, covcheck ≥90.0%, `go vet ./...`, gofmt). No live command is required or permitted for T2 termination; AC18's evidence arrives from the owner lane and is coordinator-bound into the ticket. A configuration-only diagnosis additionally records the follow-up diff in the ticket (AC19).
- **Decision-log location.** Coordinator-persisted feature ledger; the diagnosis record itself is the durable artifact; AD14/AD18/AD20 are recorded via the receipt.
- **Failed/blocked reporting.** If the diagnosed root cause requires production changes OUTSIDE T2's bound write paths (e.g. `internal/storage`, `internal/auth`, `internal/service`, any other `cmd/mcp-bridge` file) or a deployed/host configuration change, report **BLOCKED-with-evidence** naming the exact needed path/change and the diagnosis; either requires explicit owner authorization before any write. If the owner-supplied instance evidence proves insufficient to reproduce the failure locally, report BLOCKED-with-evidence naming the exact missing fact — the owner lane supplies it; T2 does not reach the host or container.
