## 7. OBSERVABILITY

(Loaded `skill://observability-contract`; applied per affected component. Components: the `cmd/mcp-bridge` startup/load/publication path and the `internal/observe` shared envelope; the `internal/config` resolver surface added at r9 is a value accessor for the gate, not an observability component — it emits nothing.)

**Logging.** All new records are version-1 JSON lines on the existing envelope (`schema_version: 1`, `service: "mcp-bridge"`), produced by the same `observe.WriteJSONLine` marshal lane the startup path already uses, **written into the shared cmd-owned locking writer pair (r10/H2) — the same sinks the refresher/HTTP `observe.Logger` writes through — so emission-lane writes and refresher/HTTP log writes are mutually serialized at the byte level (no interleaved or partial lines, no data race on buffer-backed sinks under `-race`; lock order one-directional: `Logger.mu` → pair mutex)**, invoked from the cmd-owned reload-report emission lane (AD12 r5) — after admission and after successful publication; `info` → stdout, `warn` → stderr (`config_loaded` info/success; `config_degraded` warn/degraded; `config_last_good_write_failed` warn/failure — covering the persist I/O failure and the AD22 credential-scan skip (any `ResolvedSecrets()` value match — server, embedding, client-token, or `tool_metadata`; the client-token class is a SCAN SKIP since r9; the r8 refusal class is removed), with a static message naming which of the two classes (r7/C2 + r9/C4), never the matched bytes, a credential reference, or any secret**; `startup_failed` additions error/failure — **including the admission record: stage `admission`, code `admission_failed`, static cause, riding the EXISTING event family (r11/C6); and the listener-runtime record: stage `listener`, code `listener_runtime`, resource{listener,address}, static cause (r13/K1)**). **(r11/H2, AD24(a)) The `operatorLogf` lane writes through the SAME shared pair (serve.go:198 constructs `operatorLog` over the locked stderr sink): the refresher publication-failure warns, the watch reload rejections, and the prompt-publication rejections serialize with the Logger, the emission lane, and each other — every concurrent cmd log line is now pair-held. Messages, codes, stages, and causes are package-owned finite values; arbitrary Go error text is never a field (AD1). Required fields on every record: the common envelope (`schema_version`, `level`, `service`, `event`, `message`, `operation`, `outcome`); attribution fields per §4.4 (config path, and where the diagnostic locates it, line/property; `resource.path` storage directory on the load-lane events). The `logging_write_failed` fallback lane is unchanged.**

**Loki.** Collection path unchanged (container logs → Loki datasource `P8E80F9AEF21F69F0`, bounded labels; no new label, no request/config paths as labels). Retention and ingestion-failure handling unchanged (the `logging_write_failed` fallback lane). An operator queries `event="config_degraded"` or `event="startup_failed"` with `stage`/`error.code` — one line names the file and, where the diagnostic locates it, the line/property and class to fix (document-level rejections such as `config_too_large` name the document key — `config.property_path` `"<document>"`, AD15 r6 — and class with no line); **a startup admission rejection now queries directly: `stage="admission"` + `error.code="admission_failed"` — the runtime rejected the loaded configuration; the static remediation names the operator action (r11/C6)**; **a listener runtime failure queries `stage="listener"` + `error.code="listener_runtime"` — the resource names the listener (`inbound`/`operations`) and its configured address (r13/K1)**; the startup stage vocabulary is disjoint from the HTTP lane's (`unclassified` vs `unknown`), so a `stage` filter never conflates the two lanes (AD8), and `catalog_loop` always means one of the two cmd-owned catalog-loop conditions (AD17). A `config_last_good_write_failed` line tells the operator the copy was NOT refreshed — the write failed or the safety scan skipped it (any `ResolvedSecrets()` value found in the validated bytes; static message names the class); the previous validated copy remains in service. **(r10/H2) Because the emission lane and the refresher/HTTP Logger share one locking writer pair, a `config_loaded` line can no longer interleave with a concurrent refresh log line — every delivered line is complete JSON. (r11/H2) The `operatorLogf` lines join that same guarantee; the reload-applied line's raw `os.Stdout` write (wiring.go:485) and the raw exit/validation writers remain the disclosed exceptions (AD24(b)/(c)).**

**Prometheus.** Deliberately NO new metric, label, or bucket (§4 "Alternatives"). Exception — reason: the tickets define log-line operators; startup runs before the metric registry serves, and the crash-loop symptom is already visible via `RestartCount` + logs; adding a counter would widen the closed envelope for a signal no ticket requests. Consequence: degraded starts, scan-skips, admission rejections, and listener runtime failures are observable in logs/Loki only, not as scraped series; if operations later want a `config_degraded`, scan-skip, admission-rejection, or listener-failure counter, that is a separate owner-gated addition. The existing `bridge_config_reload_total` family is untouched — the emission lane rides the same `RecordReload` call the hook already makes (serve.go:306), so the reload metric is unchanged **(the AD23 capture rides the same hook — no new metric)**.

**Operations.** No new dashboard/alert plumbing. Recovery view: for a given instance, `config_loaded` (what is served — on a startup generation, the bootstrap snapshot's path and storage directory, AD21 — and where state lives) → optional `config_degraded` (what was rejected, which line/property — or the document key `"<document>"` for document-level rejections) → normal `catalog_refresh_*`/`backend_complete` traffic; a fail-closed start shows the unchanged `config_validation_failed` + exit 1 (including read errors, AD13); a publication failure shows `startup_publication_failed` (stage `publication`, static cause) with no `config_loaded` (the failure return precedes the success report — AD12 r5); **a startup admission rejection shows `startup_failed` with stage `admission`/code `admission_failed` and a static cause — the operator fixes the configuration the runtime rejected and restarts (r11/C6)**; **a listener runtime failure shows `startup_failed` with stage `listener`/code `listener_runtime`, the resource naming the listener and its configured address — the operator fixes the bind and restarts (r13/K1)**. A scan-skip (r7/r9) shows only the warn record — the served generation is unaffected and the copy stays at the previous validated one. **A clean client-token instance (r9) shows NO warn record and a persisted copy — the ticket-03 contract.**

**Verification (bound to AC/T ids).** Valid JSON and delivery to the real sinks: AC3/AC14 assert the exact stdout/stderr writers the startup path holds (buffer-backed, same wiring as production, invoked through the emission lane). Log delivery of the degraded/loaded/strict records: AC6/AC12/AC14/AC16. **Admission classification delivery (oracle ownership settled at r12/J1): AC2/AC5 assert the classifier-ENTRY unit (`TestStartupAdmissionFailureClass` — T1, `startup_failure_test.go`); AC10 asserts the END-TO-END wired drain tail (`TestStartupAdmissionFailureClassifiesAdmission` — T3, `serve_edge_test.go`). (r13/K1) listener-classification delivery: AC2 asserts the classifier-ENTRY unit (`TestListenerRuntimeFailureClass` — T1, `startup_failure_test.go`), riding the same drain-tail seam end-to-end as AC10's admission fixture. (r14/L1) the same named test asserts the PRODUCER as well — the fake-listener `Accept` failure through `serveListener` yields the cmd-owned wrapper (name + configured address) BEFORE the entry maps it, so delivery, producer and classifier are one falsifiable chain, and removing the wrapper fails AC2's verification.** Contract documentation of the new events/fields and the storage base: AC15 (the G2 oracle is T4-owned and runnable in the amended set — **asserting all three event rows, r10/H3; the startup classification rows gain `admission`/`admission_failed` under T1, r11/C6**). Refresh non-emission, publication-failure ordering, the E1 rejected-then-refresh invariant, **and the C1 bootstrap-generation emission under inter-load mutation on the deterministic F2 gate**: AC14 (under `-race`, F6; **the degraded-branch `resource.path` storage-directory assertion rides `TestDegradedStartOrderLoadedThenDegraded` — r12/J5**). Failure visibility (fail-closed vs degraded vs persist-failure vs scan-skip vs rejected reload): AC9/AC10/AC11/AC8. Secret absence: AC4/AC8. **Write serialization and emission-during-refresh concurrency: `TestLastKnownGoodEmissionSerializedDuringRefreshPass` (T3, command 1, under `-race`) — complete uninterleaved JSON lines and exactly one `config_loaded` (r10/H2); `operatorLogf`-lane serialization: `TestOperatorLogfSerializedDuringRefreshPass` (T3, command 1, under `-race`, r11/H2).** Alert/dashboard behavior: N/A — reason: no new alert or dashboard is created; consequence: alerting continues to ride the existing container-health/restart supervision signals, which is exactly the incident class this feature removes.

**Transport/context boundaries.** The emission lane runs on the `Loop.Start`/`watch` goroutines (loop.go:168/:122 via serve.go:306) — synchronous with the admission/publication it reports; the `Published` closure additionally runs on the Refresher goroutine for every successful pass (refresh.go:455) and stays emission-free (AD12 r5); **the `operatorLogf` writers run on the Refresher and `watch` goroutines (refresh.go:455-459, loop.go:206-212) and serialize through the shared pair (r11/H2/AD24(a))**. No long-lived streaming handler is touched and no SDK connection context is assumed to identify anything; no request-correlated metric is added inside any transport handler. **The F2 test gate exists only in the cmd package's test surface (nil hook in production; boolean `memo != nil && publishedSettings() == nil`); no transport or context behavior changes. The `ResolvedClientTokens()` resolver (r9) returns values only in-process to the gate; it never logs, events, or persists them. The `serializedSink` pair (r10/H2) is process-internal synchronization around the existing sinks — no transport, file-descriptor, or context change; the AD23 admission capture is a synchronized process-internal boolean on the same hook, consulted only under the `fromStartExit` gate at the drain tail — no transport change; the typed `serveListener` wrapper (r13/K1) is a cmd-owned error type on the existing listener serve path — no transport or protocol change. The exit/validation writers keep their raw sinks (sequential paths), with the two disclosed residuals (AD24(b)/(c)); main.go's raw `writeStartupFailure` callers are :41/:45/:58/:60 (:83 is inside `checkConfig` — r12/J4).**

**Justified exceptions (reason + consequence).** (1) No in-process Loki end-to-end delivery test — reason: the container→Loki hop is unreachable from a unit test without new collector plumbing, which this feature forbids; consequence: delivery is verified to the injected sinks (the boundaries the startup path owns), the Loki hop stays covered by the unchanged collection path. (2) New events ride the Envelope/`WriteJSONLine` lane, not `Logger.Log`/Entry — reason: the Entry lane's `eventMessage` switch and `Fields` allowlist are closed and would need widening, while every existing startup diagnostic already uses the Envelope lane; consequence: the new event names appear in the contract doc and the cmd/observe contract tests, not in `eventMessage`. **(r10/H2 corollary: the Logger instance itself is not the emission vehicle — its only exported write method is `Log(Entry)` — so the serialization rides the shared locking pair both the Logger and the envelope writers write through, not a Logger route. (r11/H2 corollary: `operatorLogf` likewise rides the pair via the serve.go:198 call site — `operatorLog`'s own private mutex nests inside the pair; no `wiring.go` change.)**) (3) No new metric — reason and consequence as in the Prometheus paragraph. (4) **No dedicated skip event (r7, amended at r9) — reason: reusing the `config_last_good_write_failed` family keeps the event vocabulary closed while the static message discriminates the two classes (persist I/O failure, credential-scan skip — client-token matches included); consequence: an operator alerting on `config_last_good_write_failed` cannot distinguish the two classes from `error.code` alone and must read `message` — acceptable because both require the same operator response (inspect the config bind/bytes; the previous copy stays in service); flagged for review at CT8 and re-flagged at the next critic round with the r9 class set.** (5) **(r10/H2, amended at r11/H2; anchors corrected at r12/J4) The exit/validation writers (`startup_failure.go:130`, `main.go:41/45/58/60` — the raw `writeStartupFailure` callers (`:83` is inside `checkConfig`: a raw `WriteJSONLine` to stdout on the sequential check path, not a startup-failure writer), `diagnostics_envelope.go:12`, `wiring.go:519`) stay on raw sinks, outside the serialized pair — reason: they run on sequential exit paths where no refresher/HTTP log write is concurrent (the refresher starts strictly after the startup publication), with ONE disclosed window: on the drain-budget-expiry path the loop may still be running when main's raw final diagnostic lands, and serve/main join no goroutine, so an in-flight pair-held write (an `operatorLogf` refresher line) may complete alongside it — at most line-level ordering nondeterminism between two complete single-write JSON lines on the shared stderr fd (no partial lines; the AD23 capture is synchronized); consequence: a hypothetical interleave of an exit-path line with a late refresher line is not prevented by the pair and is NOT claimed prevented — the drained path is excluded by construction (the Start exit precedes the driver start, §3) and the expiry-path window is disclosed here and in §9 for the reviewer to challenge. (r11/H2) The `configuration reload applied` line's raw `os.Stdout` write (wiring.go:485) is a second disclosed residual — bounded to one info line on stdout, disjoint from every stderr diagnostic; `wiring.go` is unwritable and no widening exists.**

## 8. RELEASE AND RECOVERY

- **Change class.** In-process behavior + one new on-disk artifact per config (the sibling `.last-good.yaml` copy written from the exact validated bytes, **only when the AD22 credential scan passes — no resolved secret value (server credential, embedding credential, client token, or `tool_metadata` reference value) anywhere in the bytes**). No config key, wire, protocol, or schema migration; no new port or dependency; exit codes unchanged. **The AD21 bootstrap memoization, the F2 test gate, the r10 serialized writer pair, the r11 `operatorLogf` routing, the AD23 admission capture/`fromStartExit` gate, and the r13 typed `serveListener` wrapper/`errors.As` branch are in-process/test-only — nothing persists across restarts. The `ResolvedClientTokens()` resolver is in-process — values never leave the gate.**
- **Deployment order.** Standard image rollout of `registry.bullprog.com:5000/mcp-bridge`. No coordinated steps: the healthy instance first (its last-known-good copy lands on its first accepted startup generation after the upgrade — **including a clean client-token instance (r9)** — or the AD22 scan skips it with a warn record if the validated bytes contain any `ResolvedSecrets()` value — any resolved server/embedding credential, client token, or `tool_metadata` reference value); the `mcp-bridge-network` instance follows T2's diagnosed code fix — its image build, redeploy and live verification (healthcheck, one inbound MCP request, ≥10-minute `RestartCount` observation) are owner/operator-lane steps after delivery (AD18), as is any owner-gated configuration follow-up (AD14). An old-binary instance simply ignores sibling copy files (no reader). The `Dockerfile:26-28` operator comment remains wrong until the PENDING owner-gated follow-up (AD19) is decided and applied — outside this packet.
- **Rollback.** Revert the commits and redeploy the previous image. Nothing to unwind: the `.last-good.yaml` files are additive, validated-before-use artifacts and are safe to leave in place or delete manually; fallback behavior disappears with the feature flag absent (`MCP_STRICT_CONFIG` unset); a reverted (pre-T3) binary writes no copies, and any copy left behind is re-validated on every future use (AD3).
- **Irreversible steps.** None. No data is transformed; the only new writes are the validated, AD22-gated copy files, which are re-validated on every use (AD3) and never overwrite the operator's document (I3).
